## Getting Started with Antigravity 2.0
<https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/>

- [parameter] **macOS**: **Requirements:** macOS versions with Apple security update support. This is typically the current and two previous versions. Min Version 12 (Monterey), X86 is not supported. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#download
- [parameter] **Windows**: **Requirements:** Windows 10 (64 bit) — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#download
- [parameter] **Linux**: **Requirements:** glibc >= 2.28, glibcxx >= 3.4.25 (e.g. Ubuntu 20, Debian 10, Fedora 36, RHEL 8) — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#download
- [parameter] **Open Conversation Picker**: macOS=`⌘K`; Windows / Linux=`Ctrl` +`K` — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#basic-navigation
- [parameter] **Open File Search**: macOS=`⌘P`; Windows / Linux=`Ctrl` +`P` — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#basic-navigation
- [parameter] **Focus Input**: macOS=`⌘L`; Windows / Linux=`Ctrl` +`L` — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#basic-navigation
- [parameter] **New Conversation**: macOS=`⌘N`; Windows / Linux=`Ctrl` +`N` — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#basic-navigation
- [parameter] **Next/Previous Conversation**: macOS=`⌥ Up / Down`; Windows / Linux=`Alt` +`Up / Down` — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#basic-navigation
- [parameter] `/goal`: Run until the specified task is completely finished, not asking for intermediate input from the user. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#slash-commands
- [parameter] `/grill-me`: Before starting to implement, ask questions back to align on the specific details of the plan. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#slash-commands
- [parameter] `/schedule`: Run an instruction as a one-time timer in the future or on a recurring schedule (via Scheduled Tasks). — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#slash-commands
- [parameter] `/browser`: Explicit slash command controlling browser debugging behaviors in Google Chrome. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#slash-commands
- [definition] Download — Visit [antigravity.google/download](https://antigravity.google/download) to download Google Antigravity 2.0. Select your operating system below: — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#download
- [definition] Installation — You may get a notification asking whether you want to “Keep Both” or “Replace” Antigravity, select “Replace.” You will be prompted to re-install the IDE during installation, should you choose to. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#installation
- [definition] Creating a Project — Agents work within Projects, which define the boundaries of the folders and repositories they can access. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#creating-a-project
- [definition] Starting an Agent — Once your Project is created, you can spawn an agent to start working on tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/#starting-an-agent
- [concept] Agents operate within Projects that define the boundaries of accessible folders and repositories. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [actionable] Visit antigravity.google/download to download Google Antegravity 2.0 for your operating system. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [fact] During installation, users may be prompted to choose between 'Keep Both' or 'Replace' Antigravity. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [actionable] Click the folder with a '+' icon in the left sidebar to begin creating a new project. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [actionable] Select 'New Project' and then click 'Add Folder' to associate local folders or Git repositories. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [idea] Adding multiple folders provides the agent with full cross-repository context. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [fact] Each Project maintains its own isolated settings and security policies that the agent respects. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [actionable] Type a goal or instruction in the chat input and press Enter to start an agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [concept] Local Mode allows the agent to operate directly in your active folders. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/
- [concept] New Worktree Mode operates the agent within an isolated Git worktree. — https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started/

## Welcome to Google Antigravity
<https://antigravity.google/docs/home/>

- [definition] Choose Your Surface — Google Antigravity offers multiple product surfaces tailored to your specific development workflow. Select the interface that best fits your needs: — https://antigravity.google/docs/home/#choose-your-surface
- [definition] Antigravity 2.0 — Your standalone desktop command center for your agents. Start agents inside Projects, work across multiple workspaces and worktrees, and orchestrate complex tasks using parallel local subagents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/home/#antigravity-20
- [definition] Antigravity CLI — The lightweight, keyboard-centric Terminal User Interface surface. It brings the same core agentic capabilities as the desktop app directly to your terminal workflow, making it perfect for fast interactions and SSH sessions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/home/#antigravity-cli
- [definition] Antigravity SDK — A programmatic Python framework for researchers and developers who want complete control over their agent deployments. Custom build an agent, register custom tools, and implement lifecycle hooks, all on top of the Antigravity Harness. — https://antigravity.google/docs/home/#antigravity-sdk
- [definition] Antigravity IDE — The fully-featured, AI-powered developer environment. Standardize your daily coding with powerful tightly integrated coding agents, deep context awareness, tools like MCP and skills, and more. — https://antigravity.google/docs/home/#antigravity-ide
- [definition] Core Agent Capabilities — Every Antigravity surface runs on a shared, highly-optimized agent harness co-trained with Gemini models: - **Gemini 3.5 Flash** : Powering all local agents with SOTA speed, reasoning, and context window capacity. — https://antigravity.google/docs/home/#core-agent-capabilities

## Antigravity 2.0
<https://antigravity.google/docs/overview/>

- [definition] Overview — As we go deeper into the agent-first era, Antigravity is evolving to further empower developers. Introducing Antigravity 2.0, a standalone desktop application tailored for managing AI agents that execute complex knowledge and coding tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/overview/#overview
- [definition] What is Antigravity 2.0? — Antigravity 2.0 serves as your AI agents’ central command center, providing a unified platform to launch, monitor, and orchestrate their activities. Unlike its predecessor, the Agent Manager, Antigravity 2.0 is a standalone application that functions independently of an IDE. — https://antigravity.google/docs/overview/#what-is-antigravity-20
- [definition] Getting Started — Ready to dive in? Check out the **[Getting Started](https://antigravity.google/docs/getting-started)** guide to install Antigravity 2.0 and start your first project. — https://antigravity.google/docs/overview/#getting-started

## Build with Google
<https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/>

- [definition] Build with Google — Antigravity 2.0 makes it seamless to build with popular Google technology stacks. We have partnered with teams across Google to create curated bundles of primitives, including Skills, MCP servers, and Editor extensions, that are pre-configured for specific ecosystems. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#build-with-google
- [definition] How to Enable — You can enable “Build with Google” integrations at two points: - **During Onboarding** : Select the checkboxes for the stacks you plan to use. - **In Settings** : Navigate to`Settings > Customizations > Build with Google Plugins` to add or remove integrations at any time. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#how-to-enable
- [definition] Modern Web Guidance — Keep your coding agent up to date with the latest web best practices. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#modern-web-guidance
- [definition] Firebase Bundle — Transform your AI coding agent into a specialized Firebase expert that can write code, configure Firebase Security Rules, and manage live resources. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#firebase-bundle
- [definition] Google Antigravity SDK — Using the Antigravity Python SDK to build AI agents — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#google-antigravity-sdk
- [definition] Android CLI — Core tools and knowledge required to develop for Android — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#android-cli
- [definition] Science — Curated collection of agent skills for science. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#science
- [definition] Chrome DevTools — Reliable automation, in-depth debugging, and performance analysis in Chrome using Chrome DevTools and Puppeteer — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#chrome-devtools
- [definition] Dart and Flutter — The official Dart and Flutter plugin for building natively compiled, visually stunning applications for mobile, web, desktop, and embedded devices from a single codebase. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#dart-and-flutter
- [definition] Data Agent Kit Starter Pack — Specialized suite of skills and MCP tools for data engineers and database practitioners building end-to-end analytics, ETL, and AI workflows on Google Cloud. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#data-agent-kit-starter-pack
- [definition] Google Maps Platform — Equip your AI coding agent with Google Maps specific knowledge and workflows to design, build, and deploy location-aware features across Web, Android, iOS, and Web Services. — https://antigravity.google/docs/build-with-google/#google-maps-platform

## Antigravity 2.0 features
<https://antigravity.google/docs/features/>

- [definition] Projects — In Antigravity 2.0, agents work in **Projects** (previously in Agent Manager, agents were strictly mapped to a single workspace folder). — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#projects
- [definition] Conversations outside of projects — Start quick, one-off conversations outside of any Project. These sessions run in an isolated local scratch folder. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#conversations-outside-of-projects
- [definition] Scheduled tasks — We’re introducing scheduled tasks, allowing users to plan ahead with their projects. Utilizing the newest Gemini 3.5 Flash model, users can schedule messages to be sent to their agents while they’re away. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#scheduled-tasks
- [definition] Secure by default — We put you in the driver’s seat with robust security controls: - **Interactive approvals** : By default, agents will request your explicit permission before running any terminal commands. - **Bounded access** : By default, your agent can only read and write within the provided folders of a project. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#secure-by-default
- [definition] Voice transcription — Antigravity features a built-in live voice transcription, allowing you to prompt agents and leave feedback using natural speech. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#voice-transcription
- [definition] JSON hooks — JSON hooks allow you to execute custom local shell scripts at critical stages of an Antigravity agent’s execution cycle. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#json-hooks
- [definition] Browser — We reworked the browser subagent in Antigravity 2.0. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#browser
- [definition] Remote Control — Antigravity 2.0 Remote Control allows you to drive and monitor your desktop agent sessions across multiple machines from any web browser: - **Untethered Mobility** : Launch long-running agent workflows on your desktop workstation and continue monitoring or approving actions from a mobile device or… — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#remote-control
- [definition] Integrated terminal — Antigravity 2.0 provides terminal support directly from the sidebar. Access the terminal at any time by clicking the terminal button () or by using the `Ctrl`/`Cmd` + `` ` `` keyboard shortcut. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#integrated-terminal
- [definition] Version control system (VCS) panel — Antigravity 2.0 provides a Git-native VCS review panel directly from the sidebar. Access the panel at any time by clicking the version control button () to perform the following actions: - **Review panel diffs** : - **Uncommitted diffs** : View all staged and unstaged working tree changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/#version-control-system-vcs-panel
- [concept] In Antigravity 2.0, agents operate within Projects which natively support Git worktrees for isolated background folders. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] Settings in Antigravity 2.0 are scoped per project, with three main presets: Default, Full machine, and Unrestricted. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [concept] Permissions can be manually granted during a conversation to persist across sessions, allowing agents to learn trusted actions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] A project can be configured for multi-folder access, enabling agents to operate across different codebases within the same conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [concept] Conversations outside of projects run in an isolated local scratch folder with their own settings and permissions inherited from global permissions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] Scheduled tasks utilize the Gemini 3.5 Flash model to send messages to agents while users are away. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [concept] Repeatable scheduled tasks can be set up with time-based triggers that start conversations periodically on the minute specified. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] By default, agents request explicit permission via interactive approvals before running any terminal commands. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] Bounded access restricts agents to read and write only within provided project folders unless the security preset is changed to Full machine or Unrestricted. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [actionable] Start voice recording by clicking the mic button next to the text input box or pressing Ctrl + M. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] Voice transcription includes smart cleanup that automatically resolves self-corrections, repetitions, and filler words into a cohesive prompt upon stopping recording. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [concept] JSON hooks allow execution of custom local shell scripts at critical stages like before tool calls or after model responses, configured via JSON files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [actionable] Invoke the browser subagent on-demand using the /browser command. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] The reworked browser subagent integrates natively with Chrome DevTools MCP and supports video recording as webm files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [concept] Antigravity 2.0 Remote Control enables driving desktop agent sessions from any web browser while retaining full access to local filesystems and Git worktrees. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [fact] Proactive push notifications are sent via browser when tasks complete or user input is required during remote sessions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [actionable] Access the integrated terminal by clicking the terminal button in the sidebar or using the Ctrl/Cmd + ` keyboard shortcut. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [concept] The VCS panel provides a Git-native review interface for inspecting uncommitted diffs, branch diffs, and agent edits directly from the sidebar. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/
- [actionable] Perform repo actions such as creating local commits with auto-generated messages or pushing branch commits to a remote repository via the VCS panel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/features/

- [parameter] [Gemini 3.6 Flash](https://antigravity.google/blog/gemini-3-6-flash-in-google-antigravity): Free & Google AI Plus=✅; Google AI Pro=✅; Google AI Ultra=✅; Enterprise=✅ — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#reasoning-model
- [parameter] [Gemini 3.5 Flash](https://antigravity.google/blog/gemini-3-5-flash-in-google-antigravity): Free & Google AI Plus=✅; Google AI Pro=✅; Google AI Ultra=✅; Enterprise=✅ — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#reasoning-model
- [parameter] [Gemini 3.1 Pro](https://antigravity.google/blog/gemini-3-1-pro-in-google-antigravity): Free & Google AI Plus=✅; Google AI Pro=✅; Google AI Ultra=✅; Enterprise=✅ — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#reasoning-model
- [parameter] Claude Sonnet 4.6 (thinking): Free & Google AI Plus=✅; Google AI Pro=✅; Google AI Ultra=✅; Enterprise=❌ — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#reasoning-model
- [parameter] Claude Opus 4.6 (thinking): Free & Google AI Plus=✅; Google AI Pro=✅; Google AI Ultra=✅; Enterprise=❌ — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#reasoning-model
- [parameter] GPT-OSS-120b: Free & Google AI Plus=✅; Google AI Pro=✅; Google AI Ultra=✅; Enterprise=❌ — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#reasoning-model
- [definition] Reasoning Model — For the core reasoning model, Antigravity offers leading frontier models. Availability depends on your plan: — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#reasoning-model
- [definition] Additional Models — Antigravity uses a number of other models for various parts of the stack that are not customizable: - **[Nano Banana 2](https://antigravity.google/blog/nano-banana-pro)** : Used by the generative image tool when the Agent wants to produce a UI mockup, needs images to populate a web page or… — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/#additional-models
- [concept] Users can select which reasoning model they want to use within the model selector drop-down under the conversation prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/
- [fact] The choice of reasoning model is sticky between user messages within a conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/
- [problem] If you change the reasoning model while the Agent is running, it will continue to use the previously selected reasoning model until it has completed its steps for that user turn or until you cancel the current execution. — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/
- [fact] Availability of core reasoning models depends on your plan. — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/
- [concept] Antigravity uses a number of other models for various parts of the stack that are not customizable. — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/
- [fact] Nano Banana 2 is used by the generative image tool when the Agent wants to produce a UI mockup, needs images to populate a web page or application, generate system or architecture diagrams, or other generative image tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/models/

## Projects
<https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/>

- [parameter] **Organization Scope**: Original Model (Workspace)=Tightly coupled to a single local repository.; New Model (Project)=Projects are a configuration of all of the context and folders that your agents should work with. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/#key-differences-workspace-vs-projects
- [parameter] **Directory Boundaries**: Original Model (Workspace)=Agent is strictly confined to one folder structure.; New Model (Project)=A single Project can span **multiple folders** at once. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/#key-differences-workspace-vs-projects
- [parameter] **Settings Isolation**: Original Model (Workspace)=Settings inherited globally from the machine.; New Model (Project)=Projects have their own settings. Agents in a project use the project’s settings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/#key-differences-workspace-vs-projects
- [parameter] **Permissions**: Original Model (Workspace)=Broad, global permissions.; New Model (Project)=Global permissions are inherited. Projects can have their own permissions in addition to global permissions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/#key-differences-workspace-vs-projects
- [parameter] **Customizations**: Original Model (Workspace)=Skills/MCPs managed globally or per-workspace.; New Model (Project)=Reusable skills, MCPs, and hooks are managed globally. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/#key-differences-workspace-vs-projects
- [definition] Projects — In Antigravity 2.0, we are transitioning from the legacy repository-centric workspace model to a more flexible and secure **Project-centric** model. This document outlines what Projects are, how they work, and how they differ from the original workspace structure. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/#projects
- [definition] What is a Project? — A **Project** is a configuration of folders defining the environment and the scope of your agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/#what-is-a-project
- [concept] A Project is a configuration of folders defining the environment and scope of an agent, replacing the legacy repository-centric workspace model. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [fact] Projects support working with one folder or multiple folders, such as a frontend and backend repo, to provide full context for agents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [concept] Each Project has its own isolated agent settings, allowing independent customization of security settings for different projects. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [concept] Projects are composed of folders that define the directories and repositories an agent is allowed to access. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [fact] Local Folders are defined as folders that do not have git configured. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [fact] Local Git Checkout refers to a folder that is a Git repository checkout. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [concept] The worktree selector determines how an agent interacts with folders when starting a new conversation in a Project. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [idea] Local Mode is best for quick, interactive edits in your current working folder as the agent works directly in active folders or Git checkouts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [idea] New Worktree Mode is best for complex tasks to keep your active working folder untouched and prevent parallel subagents from conflicting. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [fact] When a Project is created, it starts with the default security preset granting read and write access to all project folders and permission to run all terminal commands. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [concept] Project settings apply to all agents within that specific project and can be modified after creation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [fact] Projects inherit global permissions but allow augmentation at the Project level to ensure agents have only the exact access required for specific tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [actionable] To work in a single folder, create a project with that folder and configure its settings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [actionable] To work in multiple folders, add all related folders into a single Project so the agent has full context across codebases. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [idea] Choose Local Mode when starting an agent to run parallel agents on the same folder, allowing all agents to work in the same active folders. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [idea] Choose New Worktree Mode when starting an agent to isolate concurrent agents by provisioning separate, isolated Git worktrees for each session. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/
- [concept] In Mixed Checkouts & Local Folders scenarios, New Worktree Mode spawns a new Git worktree for all active Git checkouts while operating in existing non-git local folders. — https://antigravity.google/docs/projects/

## Remote Control
<https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/>

- [snippet] Options (both scripts): install --name "my-box"       instance name shown in Remote Control — `install --name "my-box"       instance name shown in Remote Control` — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#options-both-scripts
- [parameter] Linux / macOS: `~/.gemini/config/config.json` — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#settings-file-locations
- [parameter] Windows: `%USERPROFILE%\.gemini\config\config.json` — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#settings-file-locations
- [parameter] Starts: Linux=at boot, nobody needs to log in; macOS=when you log in; Windows=at boot, nobody needs to log in — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#when-the-daemon-runs
- [parameter] Keeps running after you sign out: Linux=yes; macOS=no — back at next login; Windows=yes — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#when-the-daemon-runs
- [parameter] Comes back by itself after a crash: Linux=yes; macOS=yes; Windows=no — at the next boot, the next scheduled update, or a manual `restart` — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#when-the-daemon-runs
- [definition] Remote Control — Antigravity Remote Control allows you to securely connect to and drive your Antigravity 2.0 desktop sessions running across your machines from any web browser. — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#remote-control
- [definition] Enabling Remote Control in Antigravity 2.0 — You can enable Remote Control directly from the Antigravity 2.0 Settings: 1. Open the **Settings** panel by pressing`Cmd + ,` (or`Ctrl + ,` on Linux/Windows), or click**Settings** at the bottom of the left sidebar. — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#enabling-remote-control-in-antigravity-20
- [definition] Connecting from a web browser — To access your remote Antigravity instance: 1. Open your web browser on any device and navigate to the [Antigravity Remote Control Dashboard](https://antigravity.google.com) . — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#connecting-from-a-web-browser
- [definition] Linux and macOS — Run the installer script in your terminal: `curl -fsSL https://antigravity.google/cli/agy-daemon.sh | bash` To pass optional flags (such as setting an instance name or update interval), append `bash -s --`: — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#linux-and-macos
- [definition] Windows (needs Administrator) — Open Command Prompt as Administrator (**“Run as administrator”**) and run: `curl -fsSL https://antigravity.google/cli/agy-daemon.cmd -o agy-daemon.cmd && agy-daemon.cmd install` — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#windows-needs-administrator
- [definition] One-time sign-in — During setup you sign in once in the terminal (open the printed URL, paste the code back). After that the service signs in by itself, including after reboots. — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#one-time-sign-in
- [definition] Naming your machine (headless daemon) — For headless daemon instances, the instance name is how the machine appears in Remote Control. Three ways to set it: 1. — https://antigravity.google/docs/remote-control/#naming-your-machine-headless-daemon

## Settings

- [definition] Antigravity 2.0 Settings — Antigravity 2.0 features a hierarchical settings architecture designed to give you granular control over your development environment. - [definition] Accessing Settings — You can open the Settings panel in Antigravity 2.0 using the following methods: - **Keyboard Shortcut** : Press`Cmd` +`,` on any active surface inside the application. - **Sidebar Navigation** : Click Settings at the bottom of the left sidebar. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/#accessing-settings

- [definition] Data Collection Settings — The “Enable Telemetry” setting can be found in the Settings panel under the “Account” section. When toggled on, Antigravity collects interactions for use in evaluating, developing, and improving Antigravity and models that support Antigravity. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/#data-collection-settings

- [concept] Global Account Settings allow users to manage authentication sessions and toggle Telemetry for sharing interaction logs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [fact] Global Permissions define centralized default tool boundaries that apply to all conversations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [concept] The Appearance category allows customization of visual themes and panel layouts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [concept] Browser Integration settings configure how the agent interacts with web surfaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [concept] Model Usage settings allow users to choose and configure default reasoning models. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [fact] Customizations include managing Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers, custom skills, and Build with Google plugins. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [concept] Project Folders define local folders associated with the project, where Antigravity automatically detects Git configurations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [fact] Selecting Local in the new conversation view works directly in existing folders. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [fact] Selecting Worktree starts a new worktree in the folders; if a folder lacks Git, the existing local folder is used instead. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [concept] Project Agent Settings include Terminal Execution Policy to control how the agent runs shell commands. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/
- [fact] Outside of Folder File Access Policy can be set to Always Allow, Always Ask, or Always Deny. — https://antigravity.google/docs/settings/

## Artifact Review
<https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/>

- [definition] Artifact Review — When starting a new Agent conversation, you can choose between two primary execution modes that determine how changes are proposed and reviewed: - **Planning Mode** : The agent plans thoroughly before executing tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/#artifact-review
- [definition] Artifact Review Policy — You can customize the review workflow in the **Agent** tab of the Settings pane. Choose between two policies: — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/#artifact-review-policy
- [definition] 1. Request Review (Recommended) — The agent always halts and requests your explicit approval before proceeding with proposed changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/#1-request-review-recommended
- [definition] 2. Always Proceed — The agent never halts for manual review and immediately proceeds with executing its plans. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/#2-always-proceed
- [concept] Planning Mode organizes work in task groups, produces Artifacts, and researches the codebase before executing tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/
- [concept] Fast Mode executes tasks directly without a dedicated planning phase for simple, localized changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/
- [fact] Artifact Review Policy is customized in the Agent tab of the Settings pane. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/
- [concept] Request Review policy causes the agent to halt and request explicit approval before proceeding with proposed changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/
- [actionable] Users can add inline comments and verify plans in their workspace when using Request Review mode. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/
- [fact] Always Proceed policy bypasses the pause and immediately executes implementation plans without manual review. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/
- [problem] Using Fast Mode may be unsuitable for tasks requiring thorough research or structured planning. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/
- [idea] Request Review is the recommended policy for ensuring changes are thoroughly verified before code modification. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifact-review/

## Model Context Protocol (MCP)
<https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/>

- [snippet] Antigravity SDK: import asyncio — `import asyncio` — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#antigravity-sdk
- [snippet] MCP Configuration Structure: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#mcp-configuration-structure
- [snippet] Google Credentials: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#google-credentials
- [snippet] Google Credentials: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#google-credentials
- [snippet] Google Credentials: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#google-credentials
- [snippet] Custom Headers: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#custom-headers
- [definition] Model Context Protocol (MCP) — Antigravity supports the [Model Context Protocol (MCP)](https://modelcontextprotocol.io), an open standard that lets AI agents and editors securely connect to local developer tools, databases, file parsers, and external remote APIs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#model-context-protocol-mcp
- [definition] What is MCP? — MCP acts as a universal bridge between Antigravity and your broader development environment. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#what-is-mcp
- [definition] Add Context — With MCP, Antigravity can use live data from connected MCP servers to inform its reasoning and suggestions: - When writing a SQL query, Antigravity can inspect your live Neon, Supabase, or AlloyDB schema to suggest correct table and column names. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#add-context
- [definition] Add Custom Tools — With MCP, Antigravity can execute specific, safe actions defined by your connected servers: - Create a Linear issue for this TODO. - Search Notion or GitHub for authentication patterns. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#add-custom-tools
- [definition] Antigravity 2.0 — In Antigravity 2.0, you can manage your MCP servers through the **Installed MCP Servers** section of your **Settings**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#antigravity-20
- [definition] Antigravity IDE — In Antigravity IDE, the easiest way to manage MCP servers is through the built-in MCP Store. In the MCP Store, you can browse, discover, and install supported MCP servers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#antigravity-ide
- [definition] Antigravity CLI — Antigravity CLI supports both local `stdio` processes and remote host MCP server configurations. The simplest path to installing an MCP server on Antigravity CLI is by using the **Interactive MCP Manager**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#antigravity-cli
- [definition] Interactive MCP Manager — Type `/mcp` inside the prompt panel and press `Enter` to open the interactive **MCP Manager Overlay**. This panel lets you: - View live status rings for active, disconnected, or loading servers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#interactive-mcp-manager
- [definition] Global and Workspace Server Configs — Unlike legacy setups, Antigravity CLI separates MCP definitions into dedicated, sparse configurations: - **Global server setups:** Configured in`~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` . - **Workspace local setups:** Configured in your active project under`.agents/mcp_config.json` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#global-and-workspace-server-configs
- [definition] Antigravity SDK — In Python applications built using the [Antigravity SDK](https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/overview), MCP servers (`stdio`, `SSE`, or `HTTP`) can be connected programmatically under a unified execution pipeline alongside built-in tools and custom Python functions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#antigravity-sdk
- [definition] MCP Configuration Structure — Whether configuring custom servers for Antigravity 2.0, Antigravity IDE, or Antigravity CLI, the configuration file follows a standardized format. The file contains a single `mcpServers` object where you define each server you want to connect to: — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#mcp-configuration-structure
- [definition] MCP Configuration Properties — Each server entry under `mcpServers` supports the following properties: **Transport (one required):** — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#mcp-configuration-properties
- [definition] MCP Authentication — Connected MCP servers can securely authenticate against external services using built-in Google credentials, automatic OAuth flows, or custom HTTP headers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#mcp-authentication
- [definition] Google Credentials — Set `authProviderType` to `"google_credentials"` to use Google Application Default Credentials (ADC). — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#google-credentials
- [definition] Custom Headers — For remote servers that require custom HTTP headers (e.g. API keys or bearer tokens), add them to the `headers` object. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#custom-headers
- [definition] MCP Permissions and Access Control — Access to Model Context Protocol tools and resources is governed by Antigravity’s [permissions system](https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions). By default, unconfigured MCP tools run in **Ask** mode, requiring your approval before execution. — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#mcp-permissions-and-access-control
- [definition] Supported MCP Servers — The MCP Store features direct integrations for a wide variety of developer platforms, databases, and productivity services: — https://antigravity.google/docs/mcp/#supported-mcp-servers

## Agent Skills
<https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/>

- [snippet] Creating a skill: .agents/skills/ — `.agents/skills/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#creating-a-skill
- [snippet] Creating a skill: --- — `---` — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#creating-a-skill
- [snippet] Skill folder structure: .agents/skills/my-skill/ — `.agents/skills/my-skill/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#skill-folder-structure
- [snippet] Example: A code review skill: --- — `---` — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#example-a-code-review-skill
- [parameter] `<workspace-root>/.agents/skills/<skill-folder>/`: Workspace-specific — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#where-skills-live
- [parameter] `~/.gemini/config/skills/<skill-folder>/`: Global (all workspaces) — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#where-skills-live
- [parameter] `name`: Required=No; Description=A unique identifier for the skill (lowercase, hyphens for spaces). Defaults to the folder name if not provided. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#frontmatter-fields
- [parameter] `description`: Required=Yes; Description=A clear description of what the skill does and when to use it. This is what the agent sees when deciding whether to apply the skill. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#frontmatter-fields
- [definition] Agent Skills — Skills are an [open standard](https://agentskills.io/home) for extending agent capabilities. A skill is a folder containing a `SKILL.md` file with instructions that the agent can follow when working on specific tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#agent-skills
- [definition] What are skills? — Skills are reusable packages of knowledge that extend what the agent can do. Each skill contains: - **Instructions** for how to approach a specific type of task - **Best practices** and conventions to follow - **Optional scripts and resources** the agent can use — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#what-are-skills
- [definition] Where skills live — Antigravity supports two types of skills: — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#where-skills-live
- [definition] Creating a skill — To create a skill: 1. Create a folder for your skill in one of the skill directories 2. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#creating-a-skill
- [definition] Skill folder structure — While `SKILL.md` is the only required file, you can include additional resources: — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#skill-folder-structure
- [definition] How the agent uses skills — Skills follow a **progressive disclosure** pattern: 1. **Discovery** : When a conversation starts, the agent sees a list of available skills with their names and descriptions 2. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#how-the-agent-uses-skills
- [definition] Keep skills focused — Each skill should do one thing well. Instead of a “do everything” skill, create separate skills for distinct tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#keep-skills-focused
- [definition] Write clear descriptions — The description is how the agent decides whether to use your skill. Make it specific about what the skill does and when it’s useful. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#write-clear-descriptions
- [definition] Use scripts as black boxes — If your skill includes scripts, encourage the agent to run them with `--help` first rather than reading the entire source code. This keeps the agent’s context focused on the task. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#use-scripts-as-black-boxes
- [definition] Include decision trees — For complex skills, add a section that helps the agent choose the right approach based on the situation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#include-decision-trees
- [definition] Example: A code review skill — Here’s a simple skill that helps the agent review code: — https://antigravity.google/docs/skills/#example-a-code-review-skill

## Global Rules
<https://antigravity.google/docs/rules-workflows/>

- [definition] Global Rules — Global rules live in `~/.gemini/GEMINI.md` and are applied across all workspaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/rules-workflows/#global-rules
- [definition] Workspace Rules — Workspace rules live in the `.agents/rules` folder of your workspace or git root. — https://antigravity.google/docs/rules-workflows/#workspace-rules
- [definition] @ Mentions — You can reference other files using `@filename` in a Rules file. If `filename` is a relative path, it will be interpreted relative to the location of the Rules file. — https://antigravity.google/docs/rules-workflows/#mentions
- [definition] Workflows — Workflows enable you to define a series of steps to guide the Agent through a repetitive set of tasks, such as deploying a service or responding to PR comments. These Workflows are saved as markdown files, allowing you to have an easy repeatable way to run key processes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/rules-workflows/#workflows
- [definition] Agent-Generated Workflows — You can also ask Agent to generate Workflows for you! This works particularly well after manually working with Agent through a series of steps since it can use the conversation history to create the Workflow. — https://antigravity.google/docs/rules-workflows/#agent-generated-workflows

- [parameter] `enabled`: Type=boolean; Description=Optional. Set to `false` to disable the hook without removing it. Defaults to`true` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PreToolUse`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run before a tool is executed. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PostToolUse`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run after a tool completes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PreInvocation`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run before Antigravity calls the model. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PostInvocation`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run immediately after each model invocation completes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `Stop`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run when the execution loop terminates. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PreToolUse`: Description=Fires before a tool is executed.; Matcher Target=Tool name (e.g., `run_command` ) — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `PostToolUse`: Description=Fires after a tool completes.; Matcher Target=Tool name — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `PreInvocation`: Description=Fires before the model is called.; Matcher Target=N/A (matcher ignored) — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `PostInvocation`: Description=Fires immediately after each model invocation completes.; Matcher Target=N/A (matcher ignored) — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `Stop`: Description=Fires when execution terminates.; Matcher Target=N/A (matcher ignored) — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `type`: Type=string; Description=Optional. Currently only `"command"` is supported. Defaults to`"command"` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [parameter] `command`: Type=string; Description=Required. The shell command to execute. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [parameter] `timeout`: Type=integer; Description=Optional. Timeout in seconds. Defaults to `30` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [parameter] `conversationId`: Type=string; Description=The unique UUID of the active agent conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `workspacePaths`: Type=array of strings; Description=Absolute directory paths representing the user’s mounted workspaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `transcriptPath`: Type=string; Description=The absolute path to the persistent `transcript.jsonl` conversation logs.**Note** : This file lives in:`<app_data_dir>/brain/<conversationId>/.system_generated/logs/transcript.jsonl` where`<app_data_dir>` is:`~/.gemini/antigravity` for Antigravity 2.0 `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli` for CLI — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `artifactDirectoryPath`: Type=string; Description=The absolute path to the directory containing all conversation artifacts and screenshots. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `modelName`: Type=string; Description=The name/identifier of the model handling the invocation (e.g., `gemini-3.6-flash-medium` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `toolCall`: Type=object; Description=Details of the proposed tool call. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `toolCall.name`: Type=string; Description=The name of the tool being executed (e.g., `run_command` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `toolCall.args`: Type=object; Description=Arguments passed to the tool call. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `stepIdx`: Type=integer; Description=The 0-based index of the current step in the trajectory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` ,`modelName` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `decision`: Type=string; Description=**Required.** Controls how the tool call is gated:- `"allow"` : Automatically allows the tool execution.- `"deny"` : Hard blocks execution immediately.- `"ask"` : Prompts the user, but respects “Always Allow” settings.- `"force_ask"` : Always prompts the user, ignoring cached permissions.- `"deny_unless_prior_grant"` : Denies execution unless the resource was… — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `reason`: Type=string; Description=**Optional.** The explanation shown to the agent or user for the decision. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `permissionOverrides`: Type=array of strings; Description=**Optional.** A list of resource strings (e.g.`["read_file(/path)", "command(args)"]` ) to override default tool permissions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `toolCall`: Type=object; Description=Details of the executed tool call ( `name` and`args` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#posttooluse
- [parameter] `stepIdx`: Type=integer; Description=The 0-based index of the completed step. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#posttooluse
- [parameter] `error`: Type=string; Description=Optional. The detailed runtime error message if the tool call failed. Empty if successful. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#posttooluse
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` ,`modelName` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#posttooluse
- [parameter] `invocationNum`: Type=integer; Description=The 0-indexed sequence number of the current model invocation (the first invocation is 0). — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] `initialNumSteps`: Type=integer; Description=The number of steps currently in the trajectory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` ,`modelName` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] `injectSteps`: Type=array of objects; Description=**Optional.** List of steps to inject into the conversation trajectory before the model is called. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] `injectSteps`: Type=array of objects; Description=**Optional.** List of steps to inject after the invocation completes (same schema as`PreInvocation` inject steps). — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `terminationBehavior`: Type=string; Description=**Optional.** Controls the execution flow after injection:- `"force_continue"` : Forces the loop to continue.- `"terminate"` : Forces the loop to terminate.- `""` (or omitted): Default behavior. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `executionNum`: Type=integer; Description=The sequence number of the execution attempt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `terminationReason`: Type=string; Description=The reason why the execution is stopping (e.g., `"model_stop"` ,`"max_steps_exceeded"` ,`"error"` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `error`: Type=string; Description=Optional. The error message if termination was caused by a system error. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `fullyIdle`: Type=boolean; Description=**Required.**`true` if the agent is completely finished and all background commands or asynchronous tasks have completed.`false` if active background tasks are still running. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` ,`modelName` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `decision`: Type=string; Description=**Required.** Set to`"continue"` to prevent the agent from stopping and re-enter the execution loop. Any other value allows the stop. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `reason`: Type=string; Description=**Optional.** If`decision` is`"continue"` , this message is injected as a system message into the conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation
- [definition] Configuration — Hooks are configured in a `hooks.json` file located in your customization directory (e.g., `.agents/` in your workspace or `~/.gemini/config/`). — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#configuration
- [definition] Schema and File Format — The `hooks.json` file maps hook names to their event configurations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#schema-and-file-format
- [definition] Matcher — For `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse`, you can use a regular expression in the `matcher` field to specify which tools trigger the hook: - `""` or`"*"` : Match all tools. - `"run_command"` : Match exactly`run_command` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#matcher
- [definition] Supported Tools — For `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse` matchers, you can match against standard tool names, grouped by category: — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#supported-tools
- [definition] Hook Handler Configuration — Each item in the `hooks` array supports: — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [definition] Input/Output Contract — Hooks receive input via **stdin** as JSON and should return output via **stdout** as JSON. Field names use camelCase. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#inputoutput-contract
- [definition] Common Input Fields — All hooks receive the following system metadata fields in their input payload on `stdin`: — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [definition] PostInvocation — Fires immediately after each model invocation completes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/hooks/#postinvocation

## Sidecars
<https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/>

- [snippet] Configuration: ~/.gemini/config/sidecars/ — `~/.gemini/config/sidecars/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#configuration
- [snippet] Config Schema (sidecar.json): { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#config-schema-sidecarjson
- [snippet] Config Schema (sidecar.json): { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#config-schema-sidecarjson
- [snippet] User Configuration (config.json): { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#user-configuration-configjson
- [snippet] `schedule` builtin: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#schedule-builtin
- [definition] Sidecars — Sidecars are background processes that run alongside Antigravity. Antigravity manages the lifecycle of sidecars, automatically launching them and restarting them if they crash or error. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#sidecars
- [definition] Configuration — Sidecars are discovered by searching for `sidecar.json` configuration files. They can be defined in two locations: - Global sidecars: Under `~/.gemini/config/sidecars/` - Plugin sidecars: Under `~/.gemini/config/plugins/<pluginName>/sidecars/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#configuration
- [definition] User Configuration (config.json) — Sidecars are disabled unless explicitly enabled by the user in the global configuration file, located at `~/.gemini/config/config.json`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#user-configuration-configjson
- [definition] Runtime Data — Runtime data produced by sidecars are stored in `~/.gemini/antigravity/sidecar_data/<sidecarId>/`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#runtime-data
- [definition] `schedule` builtin — `schedule` is a simple builtin scheduler for running recurring commands. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#schedule-builtin
- [definition] `agentapi` — Sidecars can use the `agentapi` CLI to programmatically interact with Antigravity. The executable is automatically added to the sidecar’s path and available as `agentapi`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sidecars/#agentapi
