- [parameter] **General (Distributed coding)**: Best For=Multi-file SWE, large-scale refactoring, systems simulation, and research.; Trigger / Opt-in Signal=*Default* (automatically selected). — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#execution-paths
- [parameter] **Iterative coding (lighter SWE tasks)**: Best For=One self-contained change — a bug fix, a small feature, or a contained refactor. This path cannot decompose the task.; Trigger / Opt-in Signal=Explicit prompt signal (e.g., *“keep it small”* ,*“keep it focused”* ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#execution-paths
- [parameter] **Document Review**: Best For=Multi-angle critique and synthesis of papers, RFCs, and design docs.; Trigger / Opt-in Signal=Review requests (e.g., *“review this paper”* ,*“critique this design doc”* ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#execution-paths
- [parameter] **Math / Proof**: Best For=Mathematical problem solving, formal bounds, and theorem proving.; Trigger / Opt-in Signal=Math prompts (e.g., *“prove theorem X”* ,*“verify mathematical bounds”* ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#execution-paths
- [parameter] **Math / Proof (Large Team)**: Best For=High-scale tournament networks for hard conjectures and combinatorial search.; Trigger / Opt-in Signal=Explicit scale signal (e.g., *“Use a very large team of agents”* ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#execution-paths
- [parameter] `development`: Purpose=Rapid iteration; Verification Behavior=Lenient. Flags only fabricated outputs and facade implementations; code reuse, libraries and pre-built frameworks are all permitted. (Default.) — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#integrity-modes
- [parameter] `demo`: Purpose=Reproducible presentation; Verification Behavior=Moderate. Adds: no copying core logic from open source, no delegating core work to external tools, no reading test source to reverse-engineer expected behavior. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#integrity-modes
- [parameter] `benchmark`: Purpose=Thorough evaluation; Verification Behavior=Maximum strictness. Fully independent, from-scratch implementation; language standard library only. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#integrity-modes
- [definition] Teamwork agent teams (/teamwork-preview) — The `/teamwork-preview` command runs a collaborative multi-agent team designed for large software projects, complex simulations, and deep research tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#teamwork-agent-teams-teamwork-preview
- [definition] Overview — Software engineering and scientific research often run into problems that are simply too large or complex for a single agent session to handle reliably: 1. **Large-scale refactoring & migrations** : Upgrading frameworks, modernizing legacy APIs, or changing dozens of files across coupled subsystems. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#overview
- [definition] Multi-agent architecture and roles — Teamwork organizes multi-agent collaboration into clear orchestration, implementation, and verification tiers: — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#multi-agent-architecture-and-roles
- [definition] Adversarial verification gates — To catch bugs early and ensure code actually works, candidate changes must pass independent verification checks before a milestone is approved: - **Critic** : Performs independent code reviews, evaluating correctness, logical completeness, robustness, interface conformance, and adherence to… — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#adversarial-verification-gates
- [definition] Execution paths — Teamwork automatically adjusts team composition, verification depth, and agent roles based on the task and your prompt: — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#execution-paths
- [definition] Two-phase workflow — To ensure alignment before writing code and avoid constant back-and-forth interruptions, Teamwork splits every project into two clear phases: — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#two-phase-workflow
- [definition] Phase 1: Prompt crafting (scoping interview) — During Phase 1, the main Antigravity agent conducts a structured interview with you. The interview follows a simple principle: **Specify What, Not How**: 1. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#phase-1-prompt-crafting-scoping-interview
- [definition] Phase 2: Autonomous execution (structured handoffs) — Once approved, [Sentinel](https://antigravity.google#core-orchestration-and-execution-roles) hands off the project to the [Project Orchestrator](https://antigravity.google#core-orchestration-and-execution-roles), which coordinates the team autonomously without needing step-by-step prompts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#phase-2-autonomous-execution-structured-handoffs
- [definition] Integrity modes — Teamwork uses three internal integrity modes to align verification depth with project requirements. During the Phase 1 interview, your agent asks which shortcuts should be off-limits and maps your answers to a mode, which is recorded in the prompt artifact: — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#integrity-modes
- [definition] Safety, workspaces, and isolation — Teamwork includes built-in safeguards to ensure parallel agent work remains safe, predictable, and easy to audit: - **Dedicated Project Working Directories** : All operations run inside dedicated workspace folders (defaulting to`~/teamwork_projects/{PROJECT_NAME}` ), keeping your main repository… — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#safety-workspaces-and-isolation
- [definition] In Antigravity 2.0 (Desktop & Web) — Start a new conversation and invoke `/teamwork-preview` with your goal: `/teamwork-preview Migrate our REST API service from Express to Fastify, including full test coverage, TypeScript typing, and benchmark validation.` Your agent will start the Phase 1 scoping interview and generate the prompt… — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#in-antigravity-20-desktop-web
- [definition] In Antigravity CLI — In the terminal interface, run `/teamwork-preview` at the prompt: `/teamwork-preview Design and implement a cycle-accurate memory controller with lockstep simulation tests.` You can monitor active subagents, jump directly to subagents waiting for approval using `Alt`+`J`, or inspect milestone… — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#in-antigravity-cli
- [definition] Next steps — Explore related documentation and guides: - [Subagents architecture](https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents) : Learn how background subagents execute parallel workstreams. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/#next-steps
- [concept] The /teamwork-preview command runs a collaborative multi-agent team designed for large software projects, complex simulations, and deep research tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] Teamwork coordinates specialized agents to deliver thoroughly tested, working results when work spans multiple days or involves refactoring across dozens of files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] Sentinel is the coordinator that takes over once you approve the prompt, records your request, routes tasks, posts progress updates, and spawns the Success Auditor. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] The Project Orchestrator is a dedicated manager spawned to run the project, breaking the brief into milestones and handing off to a fresh successor between milestones. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] Explorers are read-only research agents that explore the repository, trace call chains, and evaluate candidate solutions without modifying source files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] Workers are implementation agents equipped with terminal and file tools to build components, refactor code, and write unit tests in focused tracks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] The Critic performs independent code reviews evaluating correctness, logical completeness, robustness, interface conformance, and adherence to project code style. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] The Challenger stress-tests code by building adversarial test suites, edge cases, failure-path probes, and worst-case inputs to stress runtime and memory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] The Auditor validates work against the selected integrity mode and checks test evidence against real command output to ensure tests genuinely passed. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] The Success Auditor is the final reviewer spawned by Sentinel that runs a full end-to-end verification pass before presenting the finished project. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] Phase 1 of the workflow is a prompt crafting scoping interview where the agent conducts a structured interview following the principle: Specify What, Not How. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] During Phase 1, the Project Working Directory is established in a dedicated workspace folder defaulting to ~/teamwork_projects/{PROJECT_NAME}. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] At the end of Phase 1, the main Antigravity agent creates a reviewable prompt artifact that you can adjust and approve with a single confirmation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] Phase 2 is autonomous execution where Sentinel hands off the project to the Project Orchestrator, which coordinates the team without needing step-by-step prompts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] Agents coordinate using structured artifacts: Request artifact captures goals and criteria, Project plan tracks milestones, and Progress artifact records live status. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] Teamwork uses three internal integrity modes to align verification depth with project requirements, mapped during the Phase 1 interview. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] If all shortcuts are ruled out, the run uses benchmark mode where the Auditor enforces strict verification rules disallowing mocked test passes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] All Teamwork operations run inside dedicated workspace folders defaulting to ~/teamwork_projects/{PROJECT_NAME} to keep the main repository clean. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [fact] The Project Orchestrator assigns specific files to individual Workers to prevent merge conflicts and race conditions, ensuring multiple workers never edit the same file simultaneously. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [concept] Each subagent has its own per-agent scratch directory where it writes helper scripts, notes, and logs rather than in the project root. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [actionable] In Antigravity 2.0 Desktop & Web, start a new conversation and invoke /teamwork-preview with your goal to begin the Phase 1 scoping interview. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [actionable] In the Antigravity CLI, run /teamwork-preview at the prompt to start a project and use Alt+J to jump directly to subagents waiting for approval. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/
- [idea] Teamwork is recommended for tasks involving large-scale refactoring, systems research, mathematical proofs, or long-running projects to avoid context bloat. — https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork/

## Agent Permissions
<https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/>

- [snippet] Configuration Examples: command(git)                       # Standard git commands — `command(git)                       # Standard git commands` — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#configuration-examples
- [snippet] Configuration Examples: command(rm -rf)                    # Block destructive deletions — `command(rm -rf)                    # Block destructive deletions` — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#configuration-examples
- [snippet] Configuration Examples: command(*)                         # Prompt all commands — `command(*)                         # Prompt all commands` — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#configuration-examples
- [parameter] `read_file`: Target Format=`read_file(/path)` ,`read_file(dir)` , or`read_file(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches absolute paths or paths relative to project workspace roots. Grants recursive read access to all contained files/folders. Using `read_file(*)` matches all files on the system.; Default Fallback=**Ask** (Auto-allowed in workspace) — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] `write_file`: Target Format=`write_file(/path)` or`write_file(*)`; Matching Behavior=Same as `read_file` . Implicitly grants`read_file` for the exact same target path.; Default Fallback=**Ask** (Auto-allowed in workspace) — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] `read_url`: Target Format=`read_url(domain)` or`read_url(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches hostnames and subdomains (e.g., `google.com` covers`mail.google.com` ). Ignores URL path segments. Using`read_url(*)` matches any domain.; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] `execute_url`: Target Format=`execute_url(domain)` or`execute_url(*)`; Matching Behavior=Actuating on web elements (clicking, typing) or driving interactive browser workflows on a domain.; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] `command`: Target Format=`command(prefix)` ,`command(regex)` , or`command(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches by exact word/token prefix. Each whitespace-separated token is evaluated as an anchored regular expression ( `^(?:pattern)$` ). E.g.,`command(npm run (build.*))` matches`npm run build` and`npm run build-prod` .; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] `unsandboxed`: Target Format=`unsandboxed(prefix)` ,`unsandboxed(regex)` , or`unsandboxed(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches commands by exact word/token prefix. Commands matching this grant will be executed outside of container isolation (only applicable when terminal sandboxing is enabled).; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] `mcp`: Target Format=`mcp(server/tool)` ,`mcp(server/*)` , or`mcp(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches exact MCP tools or all tools on a specified server (applies equally to local `mcpl` servers and remote connections). Using`mcp(*)` matches any tool.; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [definition] Agent Permissions — Antigravity uses a robust, unified permission engine to secure your environment while enabling autonomous workflows. Every sensitive operation the Agent performs is represented as a **permission resource** formatted as `action(target)`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#agent-permissions
- [definition] Understanding read_url vs execute_url Across the Platform — The `read_url` permission governs outbound web connectivity across three distinct areas of Antigravity: 1. **The `read_url` Tool:** When the Agent uses the internal`read_url_content` tool to fetch web page markdown for research, it checks your`read_url` grants. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#understanding-read-url-vs-execute-url-across-the-platform
- [definition] Cross-Platform Command & Path Matching — Antigravity ensures your permission rules work flawlessly whether you are developing on macOS, Linux, or Windows. On macOS and Linux, paths use standard forward slashes (`/`). — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#cross-platform-command-path-matching
- [definition] Interactive Permission Prompts — When the Agent encounters an operation requiring approval (**Ask** mode), an interactive card appears in your editor. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#interactive-permission-prompts
- [definition] Terminal Sandboxing (Preview) — Permission grants also apply to commands when sandbox is enabled: - Paths granted under `read_file` dynamically populate the sandbox’s read-only filesystem allowlist. - Paths granted under `write_file` dynamically populate the sandbox’s read-write filesystem allowlist. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#terminal-sandboxing-preview
- [definition] Default System Behaviors & Guardrails — When an action is not explicitly listed in your Allow, Deny, or Ask lists, Antigravity falls back to secure system defaults: 1. **Web Browsing Defaults to Ask:** Actions for`read_url` and`execute_url` default to**Ask** . — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#default-system-behaviors-guardrails
- [definition] Configuration Examples — **Allow list** — actions that run without prompting: — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/#configuration-examples
- [concept] Every sensitive operation the Agent performs is represented as a permission resource formatted as action(target). — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [concept] Permissions are evaluated across three distinct access lists: Deny (blocked immediately), Ask (pauses for approval), and Allow (auto-approved). — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [fact] The read_url permission governs outbound web connectivity across the read_url Tool, Browser Subagent, and Terminal Sandboxing. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [concept] When using the internal read_url_content tool to fetch web page markdown, the system checks your read_url grants. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [fact] In Browser Subagent sessions, read_url authorizes loading and viewing domains, while interactive UI actuation is governed by execute_url. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [concept] In Terminal Sandboxing mode, domains granted under read_url are compiled into the container's outbound network allowlist (AllowedDomains). — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [fact] On Windows, Antigravity automatically normalizes paths by stripping drive letters and converting backslashes to forward slashes before rule evaluation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [concept] Allowing write_file on a path implicitly grants read_file on that same path. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [problem] Denying read_file on a path immediately blocks write_file on that path, regardless of explicit write permissions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [actionable] When an Ask mode prompt appears, you can edit the target string in the interactive card to expand the granted scope (e.g., from a file to a parent directory). — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [problem] Scope editing is not supported for terminal commands within interactive permission prompts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [fact] Paths granted under read_file dynamically populate the sandbox's read-only filesystem allowlist when sandbox is enabled. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [fact] Paths granted under write_file dynamically populate the sandbox's read-write filesystem allowlist when sandbox is enabled. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [fact] Actions for read_url and execute_url default to Ask mode, requiring explicit approval before the Agent navigates or actuates on web pages. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [fact] In standard operation, reading and writing files inside the active project directory is automatically allowed without prompting. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/
- [problem] Unconfigured actions such as command, mcp, execute_url, and non-workspace file operations default to Ask mode. — https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/

## Asynchronous Subagents
<https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/>

- [snippet] Example Markdown Custom Agent (`code-auditor.md`): --- — `---` — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#example-markdown-custom-agent-code-auditormd
- [parameter] **Workspace Customizations**: Path=`.agents/agents/<name>.md` or`.agents/agents/<name>/agent.md`; Scope=Workspace / Repository Root — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#agent-location-and-discovery
- [parameter] **Global Customizations**: Path=`~/.gemini/config/agents/<name>.md` or`.../agents/<name>/agent.md`; Scope=Machine-wide / All Projects — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#agent-location-and-discovery
- [parameter] **Plugins**: Path=`plugins/<plugin_name>/agents/`; Scope=Bundled Plugin Package — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#agent-location-and-discovery
- [parameter] `name`: Type=`string`; Default=*(Required)*; Description=The unique identifier for the custom agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `description`: Type=`string`; Default=*(Required)*; Description=Detailed description used by the planner to determine when to delegate tasks to this agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `tools`: Type=`string[]`; Default=`[]`; Description=Explicit list of tools permitted for this subagent (e.g. `view_file` ,`replace_file_content` ,`grep_search` ,`run_command` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `mainAgent`: Type=`boolean`; Default=`true`; Description=If `true` , allows selection as the primary agent in chat interfaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `subagent`: Type=`boolean`; Default=`true`; Description=If `true` , allows invocation via the`invoke_subagent` tool. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `model`: Type=`string`; Default=`inherit`; Description=Model tier used when invoked ( `inherit` ,`flash` , or`pro` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `commandExecutionPolicy`: Type=`string`; Default=`sandbox`; Description=Auto-execution policy for shell commands ( `off` ,`auto` ,`eager` ,`sandbox` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `mcpServers`: Type=`object[]`; Default=`[]`; Description=Custom Model Context Protocol servers configured for this subagent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [parameter] `skills` /`plugins`: Type=`string[]`; Default=`[]`; Description=Skill paths (e.g. `skills/my-helper-skill` ) or plugin dependencies. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [definition] Asynchronous Subagents — Subagents are an excellent way to parallelize complex tasks and preserve the context of your main agent. Instead of executing every step serially, an agent can delegate tasks—such as running tests or performing extensive codebase searches—to dedicated subagents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#asynchronous-subagents
- [definition] Invoking Subagents — The parent agent calls the `invoke_subagent` tool to spawn a new concurrent session with a dedicated role and initial prompt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#invoking-subagents
- [definition] Built-In Subagents — Antigravity comes pre-packaged with several specialized subagents out of the box: - **`research`** : Optimized for codebase research, file navigation, and structural exploration. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#built-in-subagents
- [definition] Defining Custom Subagents (.md) — You can define reusable custom subagents in Markdown format (`.md`) with YAML frontmatter, or create transient subagents during a session using the `define_subagent` tool. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#defining-custom-subagents-md
- [definition] Agent Location and Discovery — Antigravity automatically discovers custom subagent `.md` files in the following locations: — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#agent-location-and-discovery
- [definition] Frontmatter Configuration (YAML) — Define agent metadata, capability limits, and execution policies using YAML frontmatter at the top of your `.md` file: — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#frontmatter-configuration-yaml
- [definition] System Prompt & Markdown Body — The content following the YAML `---` delimiter defines the subagent’s system prompt. You can organize instructions using standard Markdown H1 headings (`# System Prompt`, `# Review Guidelines`). — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#system-prompt-markdown-body
- [definition] Subagent Lifecycle and States — Subagents run asynchronously in the background. At any point during a session, a subagent exists in one of three states: — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#subagent-lifecycle-and-states
- [definition] 1. Running — The subagent is actively executing its task, calling tools, and generating responses. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#1-running
- [definition] 2. Idle — The subagent has completed its task, sent a result message to its parent agent, and paused execution. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#2-idle
- [definition] 3. Killed — The subagent is permanently terminated and cannot be re-awoken. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#3-killed
- [definition] Inter-Agent Communication & Nesting Limits — Agents communicate by sending messages to each other using unique agent conversation IDs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#inter-agent-communication-nesting-limits
- [definition] Permissions and Configuration Inheritance — Subagents inherit safety configurations from their parent agent to maintain security boundaries: - **Inherited Scopes** : Subagents automatically inherit the parent’s allowed terminal command prefixes, file read/write directory scopes, and sandbox settings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#permissions-and-configuration-inheritance
- [definition] Multi-agent teamwork (`/teamwork-preview`) — Antigravity 2.0 introduces multi-agent orchestration for large engineering tasks, complex simulations, and deep research campaigns. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/#multi-agent-teamwork-teamwork-preview
- [concept] Subagents allow an agent to delegate tasks like running tests or codebase searches to dedicated sessions, preventing the parent's context window from being polluted. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] Subagents do not inherit the parent's conversation history and start with a clean slate using the specified model tier. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [actionable] Invoke a subagent by calling the `invoke_subagent` tool to spawn a new concurrent session with a dedicated role. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] Workspace options for subagents include inheriting the parent workspace (`inherit`), creating an isolated Git worktree (`branch`), or sharing directory storage (`share`). — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [actionable] Monitor subagent progress by clicking into its conversation in the subagent panel or pressing `Alt+J` in the CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [concept] The `research` subagent is optimized for codebase research, file navigation, and structural exploration. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] The `browser` subagent operates sandboxed web browsers but is invoked exclusively via the `/browser` slash command. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [concept] The `self` subagent is a direct clone of the calling agent, sharing identical system instructions and toolsets. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [actionable] Define custom subagents in Markdown format (`.md`) using YAML frontmatter for metadata or the `define_subagent` tool for transient agents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [problem] Specifying an unmapped or misspelled tool name in the `tools` list may cause the subagent process to hang during execution. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] The content following the YAML `---` delimiter defines the subagent's system prompt, which can use Markdown H1 headings like `# System Prompt`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] Subagents exist in one of three states: Running, Idle, or Killed. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [actionable] Cancel a running subagent by clicking **Stop Subagent** in the panel or pressing `k` in the CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] An idle subagent automatically re-awakens to the Running state upon receiving a message and retains all context from prior execution turns. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] When a subagent is Killed, any temporary Git worktrees are automatically cleaned up while historical transcripts remain readable in JSONL logs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [concept] Agents communicate by sending messages to each other using unique agent conversation IDs, which supports flexible routing between parent, sub, and peer agents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] Sending a message to an idle subagent automatically re-awakens it to process incoming instructions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] Subagents inherit safety configurations from their parent, including allowed terminal command prefixes, file read/write directory scopes, and sandbox settings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [fact] If a subagent encounters a tool execution requiring user authorization, the request automatically bubbles up to the main UI/Subagent panel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/
- [concept] The `/teamwork-preview` command coordinates a team of specialized AI agents for large engineering tasks, handling milestone decomposition and parallel implementation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents/

## Artifacts
<https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/>

- [definition] Artifacts — An **Artifact** is a structured deliverable created by the agent to accomplish its task and communicate its progress and thinking to the human user. Artifacts include rich markdown plans (Implementation Plans), code diffs, architecture diagrams, images, and browser recordings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/#artifacts
- [definition] Reviewing Artifacts Across Surfaces — Artifacts are primarily generated during the agent’s **Planning Mode** and are accessible across both Antigravity 2.0 and the Antigravity CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/#reviewing-artifacts-across-surfaces
- [definition] Antigravity 2.0 — The desktop app features a visual sidebar and review pane specifically optimized for displaying, organizing, and managing rich Artifacts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/#antigravity-20
- [definition] Antigravity CLI — In the lightweight terminal interface, Artifacts are managed using a fast, keyboard-driven review panel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/#antigravity-cli
- [definition] Interactive Steering and Feedback — Feedback is a core mechanism of the Artifact workflow. Depending on your configuration, the agent will pause at intermediate milestones and request review on its plans or code edits before executing them. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/#interactive-steering-and-feedback
- [concept] An Artifact is a structured deliverable created by an agent to accomplish tasks and communicate progress, including plans, code diffs, diagrams, images, and recordings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [fact] Artifacts are primarily generated during the agent's Planning Mode. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [concept] Artifacts enable asynchronous collaboration by allowing users to review high-level deliverables at key milestones rather than monitoring every tool call synchronously. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [fact] Artifacts are accessible across both Antigravity 2.0 and the Antigravity CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [concept] The Antigravity 2.0 desktop app features a visual sidebar and review pane optimized for displaying, organizing, and managing rich Artifacts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [fact] In Antigravity 2.0, users can inspect interactive plans, review visual code diffs, and play back browser recordings directly within the interface. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [concept] The Antigravity CLI uses a fast, keyboard-driven review panel to manage Artifacts in a lightweight terminal interface. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [fact] When the agent generates or modifies files requiring approval in the CLI, a notification appears in the terminal status bar. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [concept] Feedback is a core mechanism where the agent pauses at intermediate milestones to request review on plans or code edits based on configuration. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [actionable] Provide inline text feedback if an artifact like an Implementation Plan does not align with your goal to steer the agent's thinking before file modifications occur. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/
- [idea] Use the approval loop mechanism to maintain strict human-in-the-loop validation while allowing the agent high autonomy. — https://antigravity.google/docs/artifacts/

- [fact] Inline mode integrates the stream sequentially with the terminal's history. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/getting-started/

## Installation & auth
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/install/>

- [snippet] Enable the Gemini API key: 2. — `2. ` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/install/#enable-the-gemini-api-key

## Using AGY CLI
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/>

- [parameter] **Auto-complete to file paths**: `@` will trigger path suggestions — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#quick-tips
- [parameter] **Clear Prompt**: Type `esc esc` to clear your prompt box (when no streaming is active) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#quick-tips
- [parameter] **Terminal Commands**: Use `!` at the start of your prompt to run terminal commands directly — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#quick-tips

- [parameter] **Reduce Noise from Tool Calls**: Set verbosity to **low** in`/config` to minimize outputs from numerous tool calls — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#quick-tips
- [parameter] **Manage Permissions**: Control permissions via `/config` or`/permissions` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#quick-tips

- [parameter] **Fork Conversation**: Use `/fork` to spin up a separate workspace and branch the conversation from an earlier point — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#quick-tips
- [parameter] **Clear Conversation**: Use `/clear` to clear the prompt and start a new conversation session — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#quick-tips

- [definition] Settings — Antigravity CLI provides a flexible configuration system to customize workspace behavior, safety restrictions, editor preferences, visual style, and performance. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#settings
- [definition] Keybindings — AGY CLI allows for custom keybindings. You can edit them by typing `/keybindings` or modifying the JSON file directly. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/#keybindings
- [concept] The Antigravity CLI uses a flexible configuration system stored in a plain JSON file at ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [actionable] Type /config or /settings to open a full-screen overlay menu listing all available options. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [fact] Selecting a setting in the menu immediately saves the selection to disk and returns to the main list. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [concept] Certain settings can be overridden at launch via CLI flags such as --sandbox or --dangerously-skip-permissions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [fact] The settings menu displays an indicator showing where an override came from, such as 'Sandbox Mode on overridden by --sandbox'. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [problem] Command-line overrides enforce the setting for the current session until restarted, even if the persistent disk setting is edited. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [actionable] Edit custom keybindings by typing /keybindings or modifying the JSON file directly. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [fact] The keybindings configuration is stored in the file ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/keybindings.json. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [actionable] To reset keybindings to default, delete the keybindings.json file. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [concept] A single action can be mapped to many keybindings within the JSON configuration file. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [actionable] To disable a specific keybinding, set its list value to empty (e.g., []). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/
- [problem] If the keybindings JSON file is malformed, the CLI uses valid parts and falls back to defaults for the broken actions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/using/

- [parameter] **Global shared path**: Gemini CLI=`~/.gemini/skills/`; Antigravity CLI=`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/skills/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#updated-skills-paths
- [parameter] **Workspace project path**: Gemini CLI=`.gemini/skills/`; Antigravity CLI=`.agents/skills/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#updated-skills-paths
- [definition] Migrating from Gemini CLI — Convert your legacy configurations, import Gemini CLI extensions as native plugins, adapt custom skills paths, and reformat Model Context Protocol configurations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#migrating-from-gemini-cli
- [definition] Overview — Antigravity CLI preserves backward compatibility with the core developer-experience constructs popularized by Gemini CLI. To ensure a seamless upgrade, the CLI offers automatic onboarding conversion alongside explicit CLI migration command sequences. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#overview
- [definition] First-launch onboarding — When you execute `agy` for the first time in an environment containing legacy configurations, the CLI automatically detects your existing profiles. An interactive checklist prompts you to choose which assets to migrate: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#first-launch-onboarding
- [definition] Converting extensions to plugins — Since Gemini CLI launched, the industry has standardized on the term **plugins**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#converting-extensions-to-plugins
- [definition] Context files and workspace rules — Both CLI platforms utilize identical workspace context rules. No modifications are needed to your existing rule documents: - **Workspace local context** : The agent continues to parse and enforce rule constraints defined inside your active directory’s`GEMINI.md` and`AGENTS.md` files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#context-files-and-workspace-rules
- [definition] Updated skills paths — While global shared skills remain in your user home directory, the target folder path for local workspace-specific skills has been updated. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#updated-skills-paths
- [definition] MCP config formatting changes — Antigravity CLI separates Model Context Protocol servers into dedicated, lightweight JSON profiles instead of nesting them inside your primary preferences configuration. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#mcp-config-formatting-changes
- [definition] Required schema updates — When manually migrating remote websocket or SSE server definitions, update the URI key parameter to match the current standard: - **Legacy schema keys** :`url` or`httpUrl` - **Modern schema key** :`serverUrl` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#required-schema-updates
- [definition] Next steps — Begin configuring your new visual parameters and troubleshooting any setup anomalies: - **[Settings, Rendering & Keybindings](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings)** : Customize keyboard hotkeys, themes, and screen buffers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/gcli-migration/#next-steps

- [parameter] **`h`** /**`l`**: TUI Command=`nav.switch_button`; Action Behavior=Focuses and toggles between inline row buttons: **open** ,**approve** , and**reject** (Left/Right arrows also supported). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/artifacts/#interaction-keybindings

- [parameter] **`n`**: TUI Command=`confirm.reject`; Action Behavior=Instantly rejects the highlighted file. The status marker updates to a red cross ( `✗ rejected` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/artifacts/#interaction-keybindings

- [parameter] **`Esc`**: TUI Command=`nav.escape`; Action Behavior=Saves your active review state, submits approvals/rejections back to the agent thread, and returns focus to the prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/artifacts/#interaction-keybindings

## Managing conversations
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/>

- [definition] Managing conversations — Resume prior development threads, scope active histories to local workspaces, and fork conversations to experiment with alternate architectures. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/#managing-conversations
- [definition] Workspace scoping — To maintain context hygiene, Antigravity CLI scopes conversation histories directly to your current working directory. When you launch `agy` from a specific directory, the agent only displays and resume sessions associated with that specific local repository or subdirectory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/#workspace-scoping
- [definition] Resuming sessions — You can return to a prior conversation at any time to continue an implementation, refine a solution, or recover from an interrupted session. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/#resuming-sessions
- [definition] Branching with `/fork` — When engineering a complex feature, you may want to explore multiple design alternatives without losing your progress. The `/fork` command enables safe, parallel experimentation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/#branching-with-fork
- [definition] Forking workflow — 1. Type `/fork` inside the prompt panel and press`Enter` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/#forking-workflow
- [definition] Next steps — Explore how the agent handles complex, asynchronous operations and parallel tasks: - **[Background Tasks & Subagents](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents)** : Monitor subagents and handle fast-path approvals. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/#next-steps
- [concept] Antigravity CLI scopes conversation histories directly to the current working directory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [fact] When launching `agy` from a specific directory, the agent only displays and resumes sessions associated with that local repository or subdirectory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [idea] Scoping prevents context pollution, ensuring the agent's semantic memory and token limits remain focused on the relevant codebase. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [concept] The CLI supports resuming prior conversations via an interactive Session Picker TUI overlay or direct command-line flags. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [actionable] Resume a thread instantly using the `agy -c` or `agy --continue` flags. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [fact] The `/fork` command has an alias named `/branch`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [actionable] Type `/fork` inside the prompt panel and press Enter to clone the entire conversation history into a new independent session. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [fact] Executing `/fork` allocates a new unique session ID and duplicates the existing workspace state and agent thread. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [fact] After forking, the active terminal switches immediately to the new branch. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [actionable] Run `/resume` to restore the original stable conversation branch if an experiment fails. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [problem] Attempting to resume a session from a different directory will not display or resume sessions associated with that specific local repository. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [concept] The `/fork` command enables safe, parallel experimentation for complex features without losing progress. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [fact] A complete walkthrough of the interactive picker and directory-scoped session cache is available in the Resume Command Guide. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/
- [idea] Use `/fork` to explore multiple design alternatives while maintaining a stable original branch for recovery. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/conversations/

- [parameter] `default`: Behavior=Pauses for interactive diff review before modifying or creating files.; Best for=Standard development, reviewing sensitive code changes, and careful refactoring. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#available-modes
- [parameter] `accept-edits`: Behavior=Automatically approves file edits and creations ( `mkdir` ,`touch` , file writes).; Best for=Rapid prototyping, iterating on trusted code, and reducing prompt interruptions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#available-modes
- [parameter] `plan`: Behavior=Prepends the `/plan` instruction prefix to analyze and outline steps before writing code.; Best for=Exploring unfamiliar architecture or designing complex multi-step features. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#available-modes
- [parameter] Expecting `sandbox` in`Shift+Tab` cycling: Why it fails=`sandbox` is an OS containment permission setting, not an execution mode; Fix=Configure sandbox auto-approval rules inside `/permissions` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#common-mistakes

- [parameter] Passing `--permission-mode`: Why it fails=`agy` uses`--mode` (`--mode=accept-edits` or`--mode=plan` ) for execution overrides; Fix=Run `agy --mode=accept-edits` or check`agy --help` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#common-mistakes
- [definition] Choose an execution mode — Control whether Antigravity CLI pauses to ask before modifying files or executing commands during a session. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#choose-an-execution-mode
- [definition] Available modes — Each execution mode makes a different tradeoff between conversational autonomy and developer oversight. The table below shows how Antigravity CLI handles file operations and planning in each mode. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#available-modes
- [definition] Cycle execution modes during a session — You can switch execution modes mid-session without interrupting active tasks or restarting the terminal. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#cycle-execution-modes-during-a-session
- [definition] Review modifications in default mode — In `default` mode (`request-review`), Antigravity CLI pauses before applying any file writes to disk and renders an inline, syntax-highlighted diff preview. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#review-modifications-in-default-mode
- [definition] New file creation previews — When Antigravity CLI creates a brand-new file, the confirmation panel displays an addition-only diff preview with a dedicated `"Create file"` header and explicit allow/deny prompts: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#new-file-creation-previews
- [definition] Auto-approve edits with accept-edits mode — Select `accept-edits` mode when you want Antigravity CLI to work in longer, uninterrupted stretches across your filesystem without pausing for each file modification. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#auto-approve-edits-with-accept-edits-mode
- [definition] Analyze tasks before editing with plan mode — Use `plan` mode when taking on complex refactoring, multi-file architectural changes, or unfamiliar codebase investigations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#analyze-tasks-before-editing-with-plan-mode
- [definition] Persist or override your default mode — You can set your preferred startup execution mode permanently across sessions or override it for specific invocations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#persist-or-override-your-default-mode

- [definition] Command-line flag overrides — Pass the `--mode` flag to temporarily override your persistent default mode for a single terminal run: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/#command-line-flag-overrides
- [concept] Antigravity CLI offers three execution modes (default, accept-edits, plan) that trade off between conversational autonomy and developer oversight. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [fact] Tool permission rules configured via /permissions or --dangerously-skip-permissions govern shell commands (run_command) across all execution modes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Press Shift+Tab inside the prompt box to cycle through the active sequence: default → accept-edits → plan → default. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [fact] The status bar indicator below the prompt input displays [accept-edits] or [plan] to confirm the active mode. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [idea] When Antigravity CLI pauses for a pending file edit confirmation in default mode, press Shift+Tab to instantly switch to accept-edits mode and approve all pending modifications. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [concept] In default mode (request-review), Antigravity CLI pauses before applying file writes to disk and renders an inline, syntax-highlighted diff preview. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Press y to accept changes and save the file, or press n to reject edits and keep the existing file unchanged. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Press f (KeyViewDiff) to open a full-screen, scrollable diff review with 3 context lines and hunk separators. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Press Ctrl+G to open the file inside your $EDITOR for manual adjustments. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Type instructions in the prompt box and press Enter to reject the edit and tell the agent what to do differently. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [fact] When creating a new file, the confirmation panel displays an addition-only diff preview with a dedicated 'Create file' header and explicit allow/deny prompts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [concept] In accept-edits mode, all standard file read, creation, and replacement operations run automatically without pausing for manual approval. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [fact] Subagents spawned during a session inherit the accept-edits setting, preventing background file writes from queueing for manual approval. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [concept] In plan mode, the CLI automatically prepends the /plan instruction prefix to prompts and uses read-only tools (code_search, grep_search, view_file) to investigate files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Use the --mode flag to temporarily override your persistent default mode for a single terminal run. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Open the interactive settings panel mid-session by typing /settings, navigate to Agent Mode using ↑/↓, select your default mode, and press Ctrl+S to save. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [fact] Modifying the Agent Mode option in the settings panel synchronizes your runtime CycleMode immediately. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [actionable] Set agentMode directly inside ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json to apply your chosen baseline execution mode at startup. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/
- [idea] Use plan mode for complex refactoring, multi-file architectural changes, or unfamiliar codebase investigations to review a structured execution outline before writing code. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/modes/

- [fact] Diagnostics including errors, authentication prompts, progress, and permission notices go to stderr. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#run-a-single-prompt
- [concept] Headless mode uses cached credentials from a previous interactive session. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#run-a-single-prompt
- [problem] In a non-interactive environment with no terminal, an unauthenticated run exits with an authentication required error instead of hanging. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#run-a-single-prompt
- [concept] The --output-format flag controls the shape of stdout, accepting three values: default, json, and stream-json. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [fact] In default mode, response text goes straight to stdout with no wrapping. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Use --output-format json to get a single JSON envelope on one line after the run completes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [fact] When using --output-format json, the CLI emits a response containing fields like conversation_id, status, response, duration_seconds, num_turns, and usage. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Pipe the JSON output through jq to pretty-print the result. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [concept] The --json-schema flag allows users to define a schema for structured output when using --output-format json. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [fact] When a JSON schema is provided, the response includes a structured_output object containing parsed fields matching the schema. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Use --output-format stream-json to receive a sequence of JSON events representing the conversation progress. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [fact] Stream-json output includes events such as init, step_update, and result with details like conversation_id, state, and usage metrics. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Filter stream-json output using jq to select specific events like step_update or result based on the event field. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Use --continue to resume the most recent conversation with a new prompt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Use --conversation followed by a UUID to resume a specific past conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [concept] The --input-format stream-json flag allows users to send multiple user messages via stdin as a stream of JSON events. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [fact] When using stream input, the response includes num_turns indicating the number of interaction turns completed. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Use subprocess.Popen in Python to interact with agy programmatically using stream-json input and output. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [problem] Unlike the interactive UI, headless mode does not silently fall back when --model names an unknown model. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [fact] In headless mode, an invalid model name causes the CLI to exit with a non-zero status and an ERROR status in the output. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#output-formats
- [actionable] Run the agent in CI to fail the job on error and save the response. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#example-run-the-agent-in-ci
- [concept] The Prompting & Interaction documentation covers how to write effective prompts for the agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#next-steps
- [concept] The Permissions documentation explains how to configure allow, deny, and ask rules. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#next-steps
- [concept] The Background Tasks & Subagents documentation describes delegating work to specialized agents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#next-steps
- [fact] A full command and flag reference is available in the Reference documentation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/#next-steps

- [definition] Background tasks & subagents — Delegate slow builds, multi-file code generation, and research sweeps to parallel background agents while maintaining your active programming flow. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#background-tasks-subagents
- [definition] Asynchronous execution model — To maximize developer velocity, Antigravity CLI leverages a multi-threaded asynchronous execution architecture. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#asynchronous-execution-model
- [definition] Managing agents: The `/agents` panel — The active agent-hierarchy and custom agent selection menu are fully transparent and manageable through the interactive [Agent Manager Panel (`/agents`)](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/agents). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#managing-agents-the-agents-panel
- [definition] Opening the panel — Type `/agents` in the prompt and press `Enter` to open the interactive **Agent Manager Panel**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#opening-the-panel
- [definition] Panel overview — The panel displays a live checklist of all active, completed, killed, or failed background agents: - **Identifier** : The unique target subagent ID. - **Role** : The specialized role of the agent (such as “Codebase Researcher” or “Database Debugger”). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#panel-overview
- [definition] Custom Agents (Markdown Format) — In addition to built-in agents, the CLI automatically discovers custom agents defined in Markdown format (`.md`) with YAML frontmatter: - **Workspace Agents** :`.agents/agents/<name>.md` or`.agents/agents/<name>/agent.md` - **Global Agents** :`~/.gemini/config/agents/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#custom-agents-markdown-format
- [definition] Deep-dive monitoring — To inspect the inner reasoning, thoughts, and logs of a specific background agent: 1. Open the `/agents` panel and highlight the target agent using`↑` /`↓` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#deep-dive-monitoring
- [definition] Monitoring background tasks with `/tasks` — For non-agentic background operations, such as direct shell commands, testing suites, or simple background queries initiated via `/btw`, use the `/tasks` command. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#monitoring-background-tasks-with-tasks
- [definition] Keyboard ergonomics — To reduce context-switching friction when subagents require manual interaction or tool authorizations, Antigravity CLI integrates high-efficiency shortcut paths. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#keyboard-ergonomics
- [definition] Detailed “Teleport” navigation (`Alt+J`) — When a subagent encounters a tool requiring approval (e.g. writing a file or running a database migration), a status bar notification blinks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#detailed-teleport-navigation-altj
- [definition] ”Fast-Path” confirmations (`Ctrl+K`) — To authorize an agent action instantly without leaving your active workspace: 1. Look at the inline status notification displayed right above your active prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#fast-path-confirmations-ctrlk
- [definition] Next steps — Configure the visual shell behavior and customize your configuration profiles: - **[Teamwork agent teams (`/teamwork-preview`)](https://antigravity.google/docs/teamwork)** : Launch collaborative multi-agent teams for long-horizon projects. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/#next-steps
- [concept] Antigravity CLI uses a multi-threaded asynchronous execution model to delegate slow builds and complex edits to parallel Subagents or background Tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [concept] The Agent Manager Panel (`/agents`) provides a live checklist of all active, completed, killed, or failed background agents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [fact] The Agent Manager Panel displays four specific columns: Identifier (unique subagent ID), Role (specialized function), State (live status), and Step (current tool/reasoning summary). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [actionable] Type `/agents` in the prompt and press Enter to open the interactive Agent Manager Panel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [concept] Custom agents are defined in Markdown format (`.md`) with YAML frontmatter and are automatically discovered by the CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [fact] Workspace custom agents are located at `.agents/agents/<name>.md` or `.agents/agents/<name>/agent.md`, while global agents reside in `~/.gemini/config/agents/`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [fact] A primary agent can invoke a custom subagent via `invoke_subagent` if the YAML frontmatter contains `subagent: true`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [actionable] Press Enter on a highlighted agent in the `/agents` panel to open the Subagent Detail View for inspection. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [fact] The Subagent Detail View displays the entire reasoning log, including private internal thoughts, tool calls, and execution outputs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [actionable] Press Esc to exit the Subagent Detail View and return to the main Agent Manager list. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [concept] The `/tasks` command is used for monitoring non-agentic background operations like direct shell commands, testing suites, or queries initiated via `/btw`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [actionable] Use the `/tasks` command to track standard non-interactive background processes and terminate runaway terminal processes safely. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [fact] In the `/tasks` list, users can select a task using `↑` /`↓` and press Enter to view stdout logs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [actionable] Press Alt+J inside the main prompt panel to instantly teleport from the current conversation into the Detail View of a subagent awaiting approval. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [fact] A status bar notification blinks when a subagent encounters a tool requiring approval, such as writing a file or running a database migration. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [actionable] Press Ctrl+K to instantly approve a pending fast-path action without leaving the active workspace or switching panels. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [fact] The inline status notification for fast-path confirmations summarizes the pending action, for example: `Subagent 12 asks to run "npm test"`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [idea] Use the `/teamwork-preview` command to launch collaborative multi-agent teams for long-horizon projects. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [idea] Customize key maps, buffering, and JSON rules via the Settings, Rendering & Keybindings documentation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/
- [idea] Enforce security containment rings on background processes using the Permissions & Sandbox configuration. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/subagents/

## Sandbox
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/>

- [snippet] Sandbox configurations: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#sandbox-configurations
- [snippet] Interactive approvals with sandbox: - **When Sandbox is Disabled** : The prompt lets you force containment for a risky command:``` — `- **When Sandbox is Disabled** : The prompt lets you force containment for a risky command:```` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#interactive-approvals-with-sandbox
- [parameter] **Linux**: Sandboxing Utility=`nsjail`; Security Characteristics=Open-source process isolator utilizing kernel namespaces and cgroups to confine CPU, memory, and path visibility. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#native-os-containment
- [parameter] **macOS**: Sandboxing Utility=`sandbox-exec`; Security Characteristics=Native system tool enforcing policy profiles that restrict absolute filesystem access and raw TCP queries. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#native-os-containment
- [parameter] **Windows**: Sandboxing Utility=`AppContainer`; Security Characteristics=Desktop security containment ring isolating filesystem permissions and registry visibility. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#native-os-containment
- [definition] Sandbox — Enforce native operating system process isolation, manage execution containment boundaries, and protect your local workstation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#sandbox
- [definition] The security model — Because autonomous development agents run local terminal commands, edit source codes, and execute tests directly in your workspace, maintaining a secure workstation environment is critical. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#the-security-model
- [definition] Native OS containment — Unlike heavy virtual containers or isolated virtual machines that slow down execution speeds, Antigravity uses lightweight, native operating system kernel utilities to create secure process rings with zero execution overhead: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#native-os-containment
- [definition] Activating the sandbox — You configure the sandbox directly inside your global preferences: `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#activating-the-sandbox
- [definition] Sandbox configurations — Add the sandboxing toggle to your settings profile: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#sandbox-configurations
- [definition] Interactive approvals with sandbox — When the agent attempts to run a terminal tool or shell command, the TUI prompt block adapts dynamically based on your sandboxing state: - **When Sandbox is Enabled** : The prompt panel offers a temporary escape option: Choosing Option 2 bypasses the containment barrier exclusively for that single… — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/#interactive-approvals-with-sandbox
- [concept] Antigravity CLI integrates a native Terminal Sandbox to restrict destructive shell operations or unauthorized remote network calls. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [fact] The sandbox uses lightweight, native operating system kernel utilities instead of heavy virtual containers or isolated virtual machines. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [concept] The sandbox creates secure process rings to enforce execution containment boundaries without slowing down execution speeds. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [actionable] Configure the sandbox by adding settings to the file ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [fact] The enableTerminalSandbox setting is a boolean with a default value of false. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [concept] When enabled, the enableTerminalSandbox flag restricts all local execution commands launched by agents to OS containment rings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [problem] By default, sandboxing is disabled (false), meaning agents can run terminal commands without OS containment unless configured otherwise. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [idea] Use the sandbox to protect your local workstation from destructive shell operations or unauthorized remote network calls during autonomous development. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [fact] When the sandbox is enabled, the TUI prompt block adapts dynamically based on the sandboxing state. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [actionable] To bypass the containment barrier for a single execution run, select Option 2: 'Yes, and run without sandbox restrictions' in the interactive prompt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [fact] The interactive approval prompt offers three options: '1. Yes', '2. Yes, and run without sandbox restrictions', and '3. No'. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/
- [concept] The sandbox provides a temporary escape option that allows agents to run commands without restrictions exclusively for that single execution run. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox/

## Permissions
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/>

- [snippet] Configuration examples: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#configuration-examples
- [parameter] **`read_file`**: Target Format=`read_file(/path)` ,`read_file(dir)` , or`read_file(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches absolute paths or paths relative to workspace roots. Grants recursive read access to all contained files/folders. `read_file(*)` matches all files on the system.; Default Fallback=**Ask** (Auto-allowed in workspace) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] **`write_file`**: Target Format=`write_file(/path)` or`write_file(*)`; Matching Behavior=Same as `read_file` . Implicitly grants`read_file` for the exact same target path.; Default Fallback=**Ask** (Auto-allowed in workspace) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] **`read_url`**: Target Format=`read_url(domain)` or`read_url(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches hostnames and subdomains (e.g., `google.com` covers`mail.google.com` ). Ignores URL path segments.`read_url(*)` matches any domain.; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] **`execute_url`**: Target Format=`execute_url(domain)` or`execute_url(*)`; Matching Behavior=Actuating on web elements (clicking, typing) or driving interactive browser workflows on a domain.; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] **`command`**: Target Format=`command(prefix)` ,`command(regex)` , or`command(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches commands by exact word/token prefix. Each whitespace-separated token is evaluated as an anchored regular expression ( `^(?:pattern)$` ).For example, `command(npm run (build\|lint\|test))` matches`npm run build` and`npm run test` .; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] **`unsandboxed`**: Target Format=`unsandboxed(prefix)` or`unsandboxed(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches commands by exact word/token prefix. Commands matching this grant will be executed outside of container isolation (only applicable when terminal sandboxing is enabled).; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [parameter] **`mcp`**: Target Format=`mcp(server/tool)` or`mcp(*)`; Matching Behavior=Matches exact MCP tools or all tools on a specified server (applies to local `mcp` servers and remote connections).`mcp(*)` matches any tool.; Default Fallback=**Ask** — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [definition] Permissions — Secure your local workstation, restrict absolute file paths, configure custom allow/deny/ask policies, and manage interactive approvals. You can also manage these rules interactively using the **[Permissions Command](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions)**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#permissions
- [definition] Fine-grained permissions — To secure your workstation while enabling autonomous workflows, Antigravity CLI integrates a robust **Fine-Grained Permissions Engine**. Every sensitive operation the agent performs is represented as a **permission resource** formatted as `action(target)`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#fine-grained-permissions
- [definition] Supported actions & matching rules — Fine-grained permissions follow a standard schema pattern: `action(target)` The supported actions, target format specifications, and matching algorithms are: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#supported-actions-matching-rules
- [definition] Global wildcard syntax — Across all supported action types, passing the global wildcard `*` (such as `read_file(*)`, `command(*)`, `mcp(*)`) matches all targets within that entire action namespace. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#global-wildcard-syntax
- [definition] Cross-platform path normalization — Antigravity ensures your permission rules work flawlessly whether you are developing on macOS, Linux, or Windows. On macOS and Linux, paths use standard forward slashes (`/`). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#cross-platform-path-normalization
- [definition] Default system behaviors & guardrails — When an action is not explicitly listed in your `allow`, `deny`, or `ask` lists, the system falls back to secure system defaults: 1. **Workspaces are Auto-Allowed** : In standard operation, reading and writing files inside your active project directory is automatically allowed. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#default-system-behaviors-guardrails
- [definition] Interactive permission prompts — When the agent encounters an operation requiring approval (**Ask** mode), an interactive prompt card appears in your TUI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#interactive-permission-prompts
- [definition] Configuration examples — Add these rules to your `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` file: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/#configuration-examples
- [concept] The Antigravity CLI uses a Fine-Grained Permissions Engine where every sensitive operation is represented as a permission resource formatted as `action(target)`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [concept] Permissions are evaluated against three access lists in the global settings file: `deny`, `ask`, and `allow`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] The configuration file for permissions is located at `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [concept] The `deny` list blocks an action immediately, while the `ask` list pauses the agent to prompt for explicit approval. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] The `allow` list auto-approves an action without prompting the user. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [concept] The global wildcard `*` matches all targets within a specific action namespace, such as in `read_file(*)`, `command(*)`, or `mcp(*)`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] Allowing `write_file` on a path automatically grants `read_file` permissions on that same path. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [problem] Denying `read_file` on a path immediately blocks `write_file` operations on that path as well. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] On Windows, Antigravity normalizes paths by stripping drive letters (e.g., `C:`) and converting backslashes (`\`) to forward slashes (`/`). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] On macOS and Linux, paths use standard forward slashes (`/`) for permission rule evaluation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] Reading and writing files inside the active project directory (workspaces) is automatically allowed by default. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] Actions for `read_url` and `execute_url` default to **Ask**, requiring approval before the agent navigates or actuates on web pages. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [fact] Unconfigured actions such as `command`, `mcp`, `execute_url`, and non-workspace files default to **Ask**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [concept] When an operation requires approval, an interactive prompt card appears in the TUI allowing users to edit the target string to expand the granted scope. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [idea] Users can broaden a single file request like `/project/file.txt` to a parent directory like `/project` directly within the prompt card to prevent repeated prompts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/
- [problem] Scope editing is not supported for terminal commands within the interactive permission prompts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/permissions/

## Projects
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/>

- [definition] Projects — Manage projects and organize conversation sessions in the Antigravity CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/#projects
- [definition] 1. Default project execution — When starting the CLI without any project flags, all conversations in the session will be in the `default-cli-project`: `agy` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/#1-default-project-execution
- [definition] 2. Opening a session in a specific project — If you want to open a session attached to a specific existing project, pass the `--project` flag with the target project ID: `agy --project=<project_id>` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/#2-opening-a-session-in-a-specific-project
- [definition] 3. Creating a new project on startup — If you want to create a brand new project and initialize your CLI session inside it, pass the `--new-project` flag: `agy --new-project` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/#3-creating-a-new-project-on-startup
- [definition] 4. Resuming an existing conversation — If you resume a conversation (whether on startup via `--conversation=<conv_id>` or during a session using `/resume`), the conversation’s associated project will automatically be used. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/#4-resuming-an-existing-conversation
- [definition] Moving conversations between projects (`/fork`) — While interacting in an active session, you can copy and continue your current conversation to a different project using the `/fork` slash command: `/fork <project_id>` When executed, the CLI forks your current conversation and associates the newly created conversation with `<project_id>`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/projects/#moving-conversations-between-projects-fork

## Settings, rendering & keybindings

- [snippet] Format and customization: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#format-and-customization
