- [definition] Setting up preferences — Antigravity CLI stores user preferences in a minimal, forward-compatible JSON configuration profile. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#setting-up-preferences
- [definition] Configuration file location — The persistent settings are saved in a plain JSON format: `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` The CLI leverages **sparse persistence** by writing only values to disk that differ from their system defaults. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#configuration-file-location
- [definition] The interactive settings panel — To edit settings directly inside your active terminal session without opening raw JSON files: 1. Type `/config` (or its alias`/settings` ) inside the prompt panel and press`Enter` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#the-interactive-settings-panel
- [definition] Command-line overrides — You can temporarily override persistent preferences for individual terminal sessions using CLI command flags: `agy --sandbox --model="Gemini 3.5 Flash"` When an override flag is active, the interactive `/config` menu displays a warning indicator alongside the modified setting: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#command-line-overrides
- [definition] Visual rendering modes — The TUI operates in one of two visual rendering modes depending on your terminal capability and connection latency. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#visual-rendering-modes
- [definition] Alt-screen mode (`always`) — This mode opens a dedicated display screen using the terminal’s alternate buffer, creating an immersive, standalone app interface. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#alt-screen-mode-always
- [definition] Inline mode (`never`) — This mode renders output sequentially directly within your terminal’s standard stdout pipeline. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#inline-mode-never
- [definition] Configuration options reference — The interactive settings panel (`/config`) and `settings.json` allow you to customize the CLI’s behavior across several categories. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#configuration-options-reference
- [definition] Safety & permissions — Manage how the agent interacts with your system and codebase: - **Tool Permission (`toolPermission`)** : Controls the authorization flow for tools (such as running terminal commands). - `request-review` (Default): Prompts for your approval before running write, bash, or web tools. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#safety-permissions
- [definition] Display & rendering — Customize the visual experience of the TUI: - **Rendering Mode (`altScreenMode`)** : Controls how the TUI utilizes your terminal buffer. - `default` : Adaptive mode. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#display-rendering
- [definition] Editor & notifications — Configure integrations with your host environment: - **Editor (`editor`)** : The text editor used to view artifacts or compose prompts (via`Ctrl+G` ). Defaults to`auto` (respects`$EDITOR` ), but can be set to`vim` ,`emacs` , or others. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#editor-notifications
- [definition] AI Credits & Feedback — Manage usage, tips, and telemetry: - **Use AI Credits (`useG1Credits`)** :*External builds only.* When enabled (`on` ), allows the CLI to use your personal AI credits for model calls if your plan’s standard quota is exhausted. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#ai-credits-feedback
- [definition] Custom status lines & terminal titles — For advanced TUI environment integrations, you can toggle active metrics or deploy custom scripts to generate dynamic status bars and modify your terminal window titles: - **[Status Line Customization](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/statusline)** : Learn how to manage the status… — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#custom-status-lines-terminal-titles
- [definition] Keybindings configuration — You can customize almost all keyboard shortcuts in the TUI by mapping keys to specific workspace commands. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#keybindings-configuration
- [definition] Keybindings file location — Custom maps are stored alongside your primary settings profile: `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/keybindings.json` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#keybindings-file-location
- [definition] Format and customization — The JSON structure maps a single TUI command action to an array of hotkey sequences: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#format-and-customization
- [definition] Restoring defaults — To revert all keys back to system defaults, delete the keybindings profile: `rm ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/keybindings.json` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#restoring-defaults
- [definition] Next steps — Now that you have configured your environment, review security controls and extensibility options: - **[Permissions & Sandbox](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox)** : Manage secure execution containment boundaries. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings/#next-steps

- [definition] Vim editor mode — Edit prompts with modal Vim keybindings instead of the default flat text editor. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#vim-editor-mode
- [definition] Enable Vim editor mode — Vim editor mode is off by default. Turn it on from the interactive settings panel or directly in `settings.json`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#enable-vim-editor-mode
- [definition] Using the settings panel — 1. Type `/settings` inside the prompt panel and press`Enter` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#using-the-settings-panel
- [definition] Using `settings.json` — Set `editorMode` in your configuration profile: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#using-settingsjson
- [definition] Switch between modes — Vim editor mode starts in NORMAL. Press `i` to type, and `Esc` to return to NORMAL. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#switch-between-modes
- [definition] Submit your prompt — Enter behaves differently in each mode, so you can compose multi-line prompts without accidental submits. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#submit-your-prompt
- [definition] Start in Insert mode — Set `vimInsertFirst` when you want each new prompt to begin in INSERT mode with a bare `Enter` that submits. This keeps the default typing experience while leaving NORMAL mode one `Esc` away. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#start-in-insert-mode
- [definition] Move the cursor — All motions work on their own in NORMAL and VISUAL mode, and as targets for an operator. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#move-the-cursor
- [definition] Edit text — Editing commands fall into three groups: single keys that act immediately, operators that wait for a motion, and text objects that select a delimited region. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#edit-text
- [definition] Operators and motions — Combine an operator with any motion to act on the span it covers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#operators-and-motions
- [definition] Text objects — Pair an operator with `i` (inside) or `a` (around) and a delimiter. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#text-objects
- [definition] Work with selections — Press `v` or `V` to select, move with any motion, then apply a command. Press `v` or `V` again, or `Esc`, to leave. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#work-with-selections
- [definition] Run slash and shell commands — Press `/` or `!` in NORMAL mode. The CLI inserts the character and switches to INSERT mode, so slash commands and shell commands work without pressing `i` first. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#run-slash-and-shell-commands
- [definition] Customize the submit and newline keys — Three Vim actions are remappable in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/keybindings.json`. These are the defaults: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#customize-the-submit-and-newline-keys
- [definition] Submit with Enter in NORMAL mode only — This is the default. `Enter` submits from NORMAL mode and inserts a newline in INSERT mode, so you can type freely and submit with a single `Esc` `Enter`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#submit-with-enter-in-normal-mode-only
- [definition] Submit with Enter in INSERT mode too — Move `enter` out of `vim.insert.insert_newline` and into `vim.insert.submit`. `Shift+Enter`, `Alt+Enter`, and `Ctrl+J` still insert newlines: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#submit-with-enter-in-insert-mode-too
- [definition] Show the mode in a custom status line — A [custom status line](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/statusline) replaces the built-in one, and the mode badge goes with it. You have two ways to get the mode back. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode/#show-the-mode-in-a-custom-status-line

- [fact] Setting the 'Use G1 Credits' field to 'off' restricts fallback billing for credit usage. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/credits/

- [definition] Model Context Protocol (MCP) — Antigravity supports the [Model Context Protocol (MCP)](https://modelcontextprotocol.io), an open standard that lets AI agents and editors securely connect to local developer tools, databases, file parsers, and external remote APIs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#model-context-protocol-mcp
- [definition] What is MCP? — MCP acts as a universal bridge between Antigravity and your broader development environment. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#what-is-mcp
- [definition] Add Context — With MCP, Antigravity can use live data from connected MCP servers to inform its reasoning and suggestions: - When writing a SQL query, Antigravity can inspect your live Neon, Supabase, or AlloyDB schema to suggest correct table and column names. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#add-context
- [definition] Add Custom Tools — With MCP, Antigravity can execute specific, safe actions defined by your connected servers: - Create a Linear issue for this TODO. - Search Notion or GitHub for authentication patterns. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#add-custom-tools
- [definition] Antigravity 2.0 — In Antigravity 2.0, you can manage your MCP servers through the **Installed MCP Servers** section of your **Settings**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#antigravity-20
- [definition] Antigravity IDE — In Antigravity IDE, the easiest way to manage MCP servers is through the built-in MCP Store. In the MCP Store, you can browse, discover, and install supported MCP servers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#antigravity-ide
- [definition] Antigravity CLI — Antigravity CLI supports both local `stdio` processes and remote host MCP server configurations. The simplest path to installing an MCP server on Antigravity CLI is by using the **Interactive MCP Manager**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#antigravity-cli
- [definition] Interactive MCP Manager — Type `/mcp` inside the prompt panel and press `Enter` to open the interactive **MCP Manager Overlay**. This panel lets you: - View live status rings for active, disconnected, or loading servers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#interactive-mcp-manager
- [definition] Global and Workspace Server Configs — Unlike legacy setups, Antigravity CLI separates MCP definitions into dedicated, sparse configurations: - **Global server setups:** Configured in`~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` . - **Workspace local setups:** Configured in your active project under`.agents/mcp_config.json` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#global-and-workspace-server-configs
- [definition] Antigravity SDK — In Python applications built using the [Antigravity SDK](https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/overview), MCP servers (`stdio`, `SSE`, or `HTTP`) can be connected programmatically under a unified execution pipeline alongside built-in tools and custom Python functions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#antigravity-sdk
- [definition] MCP Configuration Structure — Whether configuring custom servers for Antigravity 2.0, Antigravity IDE, or Antigravity CLI, the configuration file follows a standardized format. The file contains a single `mcpServers` object where you define each server you want to connect to: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#mcp-configuration-structure
- [definition] MCP Configuration Properties — Each server entry under `mcpServers` supports the following properties: **Transport (one required):** — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#mcp-configuration-properties
- [definition] MCP Authentication — Connected MCP servers can securely authenticate against external services using built-in Google credentials, automatic OAuth flows, or custom HTTP headers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#mcp-authentication
- [definition] Google Credentials — Set `authProviderType` to `"google_credentials"` to use Google Application Default Credentials (ADC). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#google-credentials
- [definition] Custom Headers — For remote servers that require custom HTTP headers (e.g. API keys or bearer tokens), add them to the `headers` object. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#custom-headers
- [definition] MCP Permissions and Access Control — Access to Model Context Protocol tools and resources is governed by Antigravity’s [permissions system](https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions). By default, unconfigured MCP tools run in **Ask** mode, requiring your approval before execution. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#mcp-permissions-and-access-control
- [definition] Supported MCP Servers — The MCP Store features direct integrations for a wide variety of developer platforms, databases, and productivity services: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/mcp/#supported-mcp-servers

- [fact] Plugins package custom skills, background subagents, linting rules, Model Context Protocol definitions, and event hooks into a single deployable asset. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/plugins/

## Code Search Command (/codesearch)
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/>

- [parameter] `↑` /`↓`: Move between individual matches — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#navigation-and-controls
- [parameter] `←` /`→`: Jump to the previous / next file group — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#navigation-and-controls
- [parameter] `Enter`: Open the highlighted result in the file viewer at the matching line — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#navigation-and-controls
- [parameter] `Ctrl` +`G`: Open the highlighted result in your external editor at the matching line — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#navigation-and-controls
- [parameter] `Esc`: Close the panel and return to the prompt — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#navigation-and-controls
- [definition] Code Search Command (/codesearch) — Interactively search the code in your workspace from inside the TUI, without leaving your session or interrupting the agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#code-search-command-codesearch
- [definition] Overview — The `/codesearch` command opens a fullscreen **Code Search** panel that runs a search across your current workspace and shows the matches grouped by file, with surrounding context and the matched text highlighted. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#overview
- [definition] Running a search — 1. Type `/codesearch` followed by your query in the prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#running-a-search
- [definition] Navigation and controls — The panel is fully keyboard driven: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#navigation-and-controls
- [definition] Query syntax — By default, queries are interpreted as **regular expressions** and matching is case-insensitive unless your query contains an uppercase letter (smart case). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#query-syntax
- [definition] Literal (fixed-string) matching — Add `-F` (or `--literal`) anywhere in the query to disable regex and match the text literally. This is useful when your query contains regex metacharacters such as `.`, `(`, or `*`: `/codesearch -F map[string]*UserSession` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#literal-fixed-string-matching
- [definition] Filtering by file path — Restrict a search to certain files with `f:` (aliases `file:` and `path:`) followed by a glob. Prefix the filter with `-` to *exclude* matching files instead: `/codesearch f:store.go Session``/codesearch -f:*_test.go NewUserSession` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#filtering-by-file-path
- [definition] Opening a file and commenting on lines — Code Search is more than a viewer — you can open any result and give the agent precise, line-level feedback without leaving the CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#opening-a-file-and-commenting-on-lines
- [definition] Open a result — Highlight a match with `↑` / `↓` and press `Enter` to open that file in the built-in file viewer, scrolled to the matching line. Use `Ctrl` + `G` instead to open it in your external editor. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#open-a-result
- [definition] Comment on a specific line — 1. Move the cursor (`↑` /`↓` ) to the line you want to annotate. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#comment-on-a-specific-line
- [definition] Send your comments to the agent — When you leave the file viewer with `Esc`, any pending comments are collected and the CLI asks whether to send them: - `Y` —**send + close** : your comments are delivered to the agent as your next message, formatted as`<file>:<line>: <comment>` so the model knows exactly which lines you mean. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/#send-your-comments-to-the-agent
- [concept] The /codesearch command opens a fullscreen Code Search panel to interactively search the workspace without leaving the TUI session. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [fact] The command has two aliases: /cs and /search. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Type /codesearch followed by your query in the prompt box and press Enter to run a search. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [fact] By default, queries are interpreted as regular expressions with case-insensitive matching unless the query contains an uppercase letter (smart case). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Add -F or --literal anywhere in the query to disable regex and match text literally. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [fact] Use f:, file:, or path: followed by a glob to restrict a search to certain files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [problem] Prefix the file filter with - to exclude matching files instead of including them. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Highlight a match with ↑ or ↓ and press Enter to open that file in the built-in file viewer scrolled to the matching line. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Use Ctrl + G to open a result directly in your external editor instead of the built-in viewer. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [fact] Inside the file viewer, pressing c opens an inline comment editor for the selected line. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Press C to open the inline comment editor for a specific line you want to annotate. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [fact] Use Shift + Enter or Alt + Enter to create a new line while typing a note, then press Enter to save it. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [fact] A saved comment is stored against that line and marked with a 💬 icon in the gutter. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Place the cursor on a line and press the delete key (D) to remove a comment. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [fact] When leaving the file viewer with Esc, pending comments are collected and the CLI asks whether to send them. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Press Y to send comments to the agent as your next message formatted as <file>:<line>: <comment> and close the viewer. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Press N to discard comments and close the viewer without sending them. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/
- [actionable] Press Esc while in the comment collection prompt to cancel and keep editing. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/codesearch/

## Diff Command (/diff)
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/>

- [parameter] `Tab` /`→` /`←`: Cycle modes (VCS → Turn → Commit) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-list-view-vcs-turn-modes
- [parameter] `↑` /`↓` (or`J` /`K` ): Navigate file list — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-list-view-vcs-turn-modes
- [parameter] `Enter`: Open selected file’s Detail View — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-list-view-vcs-turn-modes
- [parameter] `Esc`: Exit Diff Viewer — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-list-view-vcs-turn-modes
- [parameter] `↑` /`↓`: Scroll diff content — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-detail-view
- [parameter] `PgUp` /`PgDn`: Scroll diff by page — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-detail-view
- [parameter] `J` /`K` (or`→` /`←` ): Switch to next / previous file — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-detail-view
- [parameter] `N` /`Shift` +`N`: Jump to next / previous diff hunk — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-detail-view
- [parameter] `C`: Add/edit comment on the current line — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-detail-view
- [parameter] `D`: Delete comment on the current line — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-detail-view
- [parameter] `Esc`: Return to File List View — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#file-detail-view
- [parameter] `↑` /`↓`: Navigate commit history — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#commit-tree-view-commit-mode
- [parameter] `←` /`→`: Navigate to adjacent branches in the graph — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#commit-tree-view-commit-mode
- [parameter] `Enter`: Load diff for the selected commit — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#commit-tree-view-commit-mode
- [parameter] `Esc`: Exit Diff Viewer — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#commit-tree-view-commit-mode
- [parameter] `Shift` +`Y`: Exit and send comments to the agent — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#exit-confirmation-screen
- [parameter] `Shift` +`N`: Exit and discard comments — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#exit-confirmation-screen
- [parameter] `Esc`: Return to File List View — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#exit-confirmation-screen
- [definition] Diff Command (/diff) — View and review workspace changes, commit history, and agent turn diffs interactively within the TUI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#diff-command-diff
- [definition] Overview — The `/diff` command opens the **Interactive Diff Viewer**, a full-screen panel that allows you to inspect changes in your workspace and conversation history. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#overview
- [definition] Interactive Diff Viewer Panels — To open the Diff Viewer: 1. Type `/diff` in the prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#interactive-diff-viewer-panels
- [definition] Navigation and Controls — The Diff Viewer operates in three modes, which you can cycle through using `Tab` (or `→` / `←` arrow keys): - **VCS Mode** : Shows a list of all modified and untracked files in your active workspace. - Supports Git, Mercurial (Hg), and Jujutsu (JJ) automatically. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#navigation-and-controls
- [definition] Step-by-Step Walkthrough — Here is how to use the Diff Viewer to review changes, add comments, and steer the agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#step-by-step-walkthrough
- [definition] 1. Reviewing Workspace Changes (VCS Mode) — When you run `/diff`, it opens in **VCS Mode** by default (if you have uncommitted changes). You will see a list of modified and untracked files: Press `Enter` on a file to open its **Detail View**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#1-reviewing-workspace-changes-vcs-mode
- [definition] 2. Adding Comments and Steering the Agent — While in the Detail View, you can review the code and write feedback directly onto specific lines. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#2-adding-comments-and-steering-the-agent
- [definition] 3. Reviewing Turn History (Turn Mode) — Press `Tab` to switch to **Turn Mode**. This groups changes by the conversation turn in which they were introduced, allowing you to see exactly what the agent did in previous steps: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#3-reviewing-turn-history-turn-mode
- [definition] 4. Navigating the Commit Tree (Commit Mode) — Press `Tab` again to switch to **Commit Mode**. This renders the repository’s commit history as an interactive graph. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/#4-navigating-the-commit-tree-commit-mode
- [concept] The /diff command opens the Interactive Diff Viewer, a full-screen panel for inspecting workspace changes, conversation history, and commit diffs interactively. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [fact] The viewer supports three distinct modes: VCS Mode, Turn Mode, and Commit Mode. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] To open the Diff Viewer, type /diff in the prompt box and press Enter. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [concept] Users can cycle through the three modes using Tab or the left/right arrow keys. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [fact] VCS Mode shows a list of all modified and untracked files in the active workspace. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [fact] The VCS Mode automatically supports Git, Mercurial (Hg), and Jujutsu (JJ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] In VCS Mode, use Up/Down arrows to navigate the file list and press Enter to open the detail view. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [concept] Turn Mode displays changes introduced by the agent in each turn of the current conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] In Turn Mode, use Up/Down arrows to navigate and press Enter to view details. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [concept] Commit Mode renders an interactive commit graph/tree of the repository. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] In Commit Mode, use Up/Down arrows to navigate the chain and Left/Right arrows to hop between adjacent branches. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] Press Enter in Commit Mode to load the diff for the selected commit. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [fact] The Diff Viewer opens in VCS Mode by default if there are uncommitted changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] In the Detail View, use Up/Down arrows to scroll through the unified diff. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] Use J/K or Left/Right arrows to quickly swap between files without returning to the list. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] Press N or Shift+N to jump to the next or previous diff hunk. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] To add a comment, scroll to the target line and press C to open the Comment Input overlay. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] Type feedback in the input and press Enter to save, or Esc to cancel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [fact] Saved comments are marked in the diff and can be deleted by highlighting the line and pressing D. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] Press Esc to return to the file list, then press Esc again to exit /diff. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [fact] If unsaved comments exist upon exit, a confirmation screen appears. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] Press Shift+Y to approve and exit; comments are formatted and sent as the next prompt to steer the agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [actionable] Press Shift+N to reject and exit, discarding all unsaved comments. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [concept] Turn Mode groups changes by the conversation turn in which they were introduced. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/
- [idea] Use Turn Mode to review the agent's work step-by-step and see exactly what was done in previous steps. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/diff/

## Permissions Command (/permissions)
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/>

- [definition] Permissions Command (/permissions) — Manage your fine-grained agent permission rules interactively within the TUI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#permissions-command-permissions
- [definition] Overview — Antigravity CLI uses a fine-grained permissions engine to secure your workstation. While you can configure these rules manually in your settings file, the `/permissions` command opens an interactive **Permissions Manager** TUI panel to view, add, edit, and delete rules live. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#overview
- [definition] Managing permissions interactively — To open the Permissions Manager: 1. Type `/permissions` in the prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#managing-permissions-interactively
- [definition] Navigation and controls — The Permissions Manager operates in three panels: 1. **Scope Picker** : Select the configuration scope you want to edit: - **Project** : Rules applying only to the active project (disabled if no project is open). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#navigation-and-controls
- [definition] Step-by-step walkthrough — Here is how to view, add, edit, and delete rules live in the TUI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#step-by-step-walkthrough
- [definition] 1. Selecting a scope and viewing rules — When you run `/permissions`, you first see the **Scope Picker**. Select **Global** to manage your global rules: Press `Enter` to open the **Rule Viewer** for the selected scope. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#1-selecting-a-scope-and-viewing-rules
- [definition] 2. Adding a permission rule — To allow the agent to run `git` commands automatically without prompting: 1. In the Rule Viewer, press `A` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#2-adding-a-permission-rule
- [definition] 3. Editing a permission rule — If you want to restrict the agent so it can only run `git diff` automatically, you can edit the rule: 1. In the Rule Viewer, use `↑` /`↓` to highlight`command(git)` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#3-editing-a-permission-rule
- [definition] 4. Deleting a permission rule — To remove a rule and revert to prompting for those actions: 1. In the Rule Viewer, highlight the rule you want to delete (e.g., `command(git diff)` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/#4-deleting-a-permission-rule
- [concept] The /permissions command opens an interactive Permissions Manager TUI panel to view, add, edit, and delete fine-grained agent permission rules live. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [actionable] To open the Permissions Manager, type /permissions in the prompt box and press Enter. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [concept] The Permissions Manager operates in three panels: Scope Picker, Rule Viewer, and Add/Edit Rule input field. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [fact] The Project scope applies rules only to the active project and is disabled if no project is open. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [concept] Shared rules are shared across all Antigravity products, while Global rules apply to all sessions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [actionable] Use ↑ / ↓ (or J / K) to navigate the Scope Picker, Enter to select, and Esc to exit. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [actionable] In the Rule Viewer, switch between allowlist, denylist, and asklist tabs using ← / → (or Tab). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [actionable] Press A to add a new rule, E (or Ctrl + G) to edit the highlighted rule, and D (or Backspace) to delete it. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [fact] Rules must follow the action(target) format, such as command(git). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [actionable] Press Enter to validate and save a rule in the Add/Edit Rule panel, or press Esc to cancel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [fact] Editing a rule replaces the old rule with the new one upon saving. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [fact] Deleting a rule immediately removes it from the list, reverting to prompting for those actions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/
- [idea] For details on the permission engine, supported actions, and manual configuration, refer to the Permissions Guide. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/permissions/

- [definition] Interactive Session Picker — To open the Session Picker inside the TUI: 1. Type `/resume` (or aliases`/switch` ,`/conversation` ) in the prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#interactive-session-picker
- [definition] 1. Navigating and Searching Conversations — The Session Picker displays a list of past conversations sorted by recency (newest first). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#1-navigating-and-searching-conversations
- [definition] 2. Renaming a Conversation — To keep your history organized, you can rename conversations directly within the picker: 1. Use `↑` /`↓` to highlight the conversation you want to rename. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#2-renaming-a-conversation
- [definition] 3. Deleting a Conversation — To clean up obsolete threads: 1. Highlight the target conversation in the list. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#3-deleting-a-conversation

With the Session Picker open, press `Tab` to switch from the**CLI** tab to the**Antigravity** tab. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#4-importing-from-antigravity-20
- [definition] Command-Line Shortcuts — You can bypass the TUI picker and resume sessions directly when launching `agy` from your host shell. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#command-line-shortcuts
- [definition] Quick Resume Last Session (`-c` / `--continue`) — To instantly resume the single most recent conversation associated with your active workspace: `agy -c` *(Alternative: `agy --continue`)* — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#quick-resume-last-session-c-continue
- [definition] Resume Specific Session (`--conversation`) — To load a specific conversation directly by its unique ID: `agy --conversation <conversation-id>` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#resume-specific-session-conversation
- [definition] Under the Hood: The Session Cache — When you use the `-c` / `--continue` flag, the CLI resolves the target session using a local workspace-keyed cache. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/resume/#under-the-hood-the-session-cache

## Status Line Command (/statusline)

- [definition] Toggle Status Line — Type `/statusline` with no arguments to toggle the status line on and off: `/statusline` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/statusline/#toggle-status-line
- [definition] Enable or Disable Explicitly — You can explicitly enable or disable the status line: - **Enable** :`/statusline on` or`/statusline enable` - **Disable** :`/statusline off` or`/statusline disable` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/statusline/#enable-or-disable-explicitly
- [definition] Configure a Custom Command — To route the agent state JSON payload to a custom script and render its output in the status line, pass the command as an argument: `/statusline ~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/statusline.sh` This immediately updates your settings and starts running the script to render the status line. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/statusline/#configure-a-custom-command
- [definition] Revert to Default — To delete your custom command configuration and revert to the built-in default status line: `/statusline delete` *(Note: `/statusline reset` is also supported).* — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/statusline/#revert-to-default
- [definition] Show Help — To view the quick command reference: `/statusline help` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/statusline/#show-help

## Voice Dictation (/voice)
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/>

- [parameter] `Enter` (or`F5` ): Stop recording and insert the transcript into the prompt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#dictation-controls
- [parameter] `Esc`: Discard the recording and restore previous prompt contents. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#dictation-controls
- [parameter] `Ctrl` +`Z`*(after)*: Revert the entire dictation as a single undo operation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#dictation-controls
- [definition] Overview — The `/voice` command (alias `/record`) records audio from your microphone and streams real-time transcripts directly into the prompt box as you speak. You can also start dictation at any time using the `F5` key. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#overview
- [definition] Dictating a prompt — To dictate a prompt into the terminal, follow these steps: 1. Press `F5` , or type`/voice` in the prompt box and press`Enter` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#dictating-a-prompt
- [definition] Dictation controls — The following keyboard shortcuts control active dictation sessions. All other keystrokes are ignored during recording to prevent accidental input: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#dictation-controls
- [definition] Availability — Voice dictation is subject to the following operating constraints: - Dictation requires an interactive TUI session and is disabled in non-interactive print mode (`--print` ). - Dictation is not currently supported for business or enterprise accounts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#availability
- [definition] Voice over SSH — When the CLI runs on a remote machine over Secure Shell (SSH), the remote environment lacks direct access to your local audio hardware. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#voice-over-ssh
- [definition] 1. Serve your local microphone — Start the microphone server on your local machine: `agy mic-serve` Keep this process running while dictating. By default, `mic-serve` listens on `127.0.0.1:4713` and streams audio from your default input device to connected clients. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#1-serve-your-local-microphone
- [definition] 2. Open a reverse tunnel — From your local machine, forward the local audio port to the remote machine by opening a reverse SSH tunnel: `ssh -R 24713:localhost:4713 <remote-machine>` The tunnel must remain active during dictation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#2-open-a-reverse-tunnel
- [definition] 3. Start the CLI with `ANTIGRAVITY_MIC` — In your remote SSH session, launch the CLI with the `ANTIGRAVITY_MIC` environment variable set to the forwarded tunnel port. For example: `ANTIGRAVITY_MIC=localhost:24713 agy` If you are using a Windows remote host and use PowerShell, set the environment variable and start the CLI: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#3-start-the-cli-with-antigravity-mic
- [definition] 4. Verify the tunnel connection — If no connection log appears in the `mic-serve` output when dictating, test whether the forwarded port is reachable by capturing a raw audio sample on the remote host. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/commands/voice/#4-verify-the-tunnel-connection

## Best practices for Antigravity CLI
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/>

- [snippet] Establish structured permissions: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#establish-structured-permissions
- [definition] Best practices for Antigravity CLI — Master the workflows, prompt architectures, and local configuration choices to maximize agent velocity while maintaining robust control. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#best-practices-for-antigravity-cli
- [definition] Establish verification loops — The single most effective way to ensure reliable, correct modifications from an autonomous agent is to provide the agent with a local verification mechanism (such as unit tests, build commands, or formatting scripts). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#establish-verification-loops
- [definition] Explore, plan, then execute — Autonomous local agents operate with highest accuracy when complex changes are partitioned into distinct exploration, planning, and execution phases. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#explore-plan-then-execute
- [definition] Enrich your prompting context — Give local agents high-fidelity indicators to narrow down reasoning boundaries and minimize token overhead. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#enrich-your-prompting-context
- [definition] Target file autocompletion — Type `@` within your prompt box to trigger the **Interactive Path Suggestion** overlay. Highlighting and selecting a path imports the absolute workspace file path directly into your prompt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#target-file-autocompletion
- [definition] Attaching visual evidence — If debugging visual UI issues, rendering bugs, or frontend layout inconsistencies, capture a screenshot or video recording, copy it, and press `ctrl+v` inside the prompt box to attach it. The agent will consult the media file to diagnose the issue. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#attaching-visual-evidence
- [definition] Configure your workspace environment — Optimize your local workstation rules and security boundaries to match your engineering flow. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#configure-your-workspace-environment
- [definition] Write a codebase rule file — Create a `GEMINI.md` or `AGENTS.md` file at your workspace root to outline specific directory standards, styling paradigms, test command parameters, and deprecation warnings. The agent automatically parses these rules on startup and consults them before suggesting changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#write-a-codebase-rule-file
- [definition] Establish structured permissions — Tune your safety barriers in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` based on your project risk level: - **`request-review`** (Default): Prompts you before executing any write operations, bash commands, or remote network calls. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#establish-structured-permissions
- [definition] Manage TUI sessions proactively — Use active session navigation tools to recover from engineering dead-ends or course-correct intermediate agent loops. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#manage-tui-sessions-proactively
- [definition] Course-correct early (`esc`) — If you watch an agent execute an incorrect search pattern or write code that deviates from your intentions, press the global escape hatch key `esc` immediately to interrupt the turn and regain focus of a clean prompt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#course-correct-early-esc
- [definition] Rewind history with `/rewind` — If an agent has made several successive changes that introduce build errors, you do not need to discard the session. Type `/rewind` (or `/undo`) to roll back your conversation thread to a previous stable checkout. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#rewind-history-with-rewind
- [definition] Branch experiments with `/fork` — If you are unsure of the best implementation path: 1. Reach a stable baseline thread. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#branch-experiments-with-fork
- [definition] Automate and script — Antigravity CLI is designed to operate seamlessly within standard shell pipeline tools. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#automate-and-script
- [definition] Run non-interactive commands (`-p`) — To automate quick queries or integrate agents into git hooks, use the one-shot prompt flag `-p`: `agy -p "Review this git diff and draft a conventional commit message" --cwd $(pwd)` — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#run-non-interactive-commands-p
- [definition] Fan out using parallel subagents — For large-scale sweeps or multi-file refactoring, direct the primary agent to spawn concurrent background subagents. The agent manager handles background threads autonomously while you continue working on your primary screen. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#fan-out-using-parallel-subagents
- [definition] Related resources — Learn how to configure settings and customize visual layouts: - **[Settings, Rendering & Keybindings](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/settings)** : Customize keyboard hotkeys and buffers. - **[Permissions & Sandbox](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/sandbox)** : Enforce filesystem containment. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/#related-resources
- [concept] Establishing verification loops, such as unit tests or build commands, is the most effective way to ensure reliable modifications from an autonomous agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Before asking an agent to implement a code change, ensure your workspace directory has a test suite ready or direct the agent to write one first. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [idea] Instruct the agent to run local test commands after proposing code changes and iterate on the outputs automatically. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [concept] Autonomous local agents operate with highest accuracy when complex changes are partitioned into distinct exploration, planning, and execution phases. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Ask the agent to explain how a codebase resolves a problem or defines an interface during the exploration phase before writing changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Request an implementation plan listing targeted files, required dependencies, and logic overrides during the planning phase. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Direct the agent to apply edits only after you approve the structured implementation plan. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [concept] Target file autocompletion is triggered by typing `@` within the prompt box, which opens an Interactive Path Suggestion overlay. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Highlight and select a path in the overlay to import the absolute workspace file path directly into your prompt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] To attach visual evidence for debugging UI issues, copy a screenshot or video and press `ctrl+v` inside the prompt box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Create a `GEMINI.md` or `AGENTS.md` file at your workspace root to outline directory standards, styling paradigms, and deprecation warnings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [fact] The agent automatically parses rules in `GEMINI.md` or `AGENTS.md` on startup and consults them before suggesting changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [concept] Safety barriers are tuned in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` based on project risk level using three modes: `request-review`, `proceed-in-sandbox`, and `strict`. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [fact] The `request-review` mode is the default setting, prompting the user before executing write operations, bash commands, or remote network calls. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [fact] The `proceed-in-sandbox` mode restricts all terminal executions to a secure sandbox containment ring while allowing safe commands to execute autonomously. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [fact] The `strict` mode always prompts for all non-read operations, providing complete line-by-line transparency. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Press the global escape hatch key `esc` immediately to interrupt an agent turn if it executes an incorrect search pattern or deviates from intentions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Type `/rewind` or `/undo` to roll back the conversation thread to a previous stable checkout if successive changes introduce build errors. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Type `/fork` at a stable baseline thread to spin up a duplicate parallel session for testing speculative code modifications. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Run `/resume` to swap back to your stable main branch if the branched experiment approach fails. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [concept] Antigravity CLI is designed to operate within standard shell pipeline tools using the one-shot prompt flag `-p` for automation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [actionable] Use `agy -p "Review this git diff and draft a conventional commit message" --cwd $(pwd)` to automate quick queries or integrate agents into git hooks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/
- [idea] For large-scale sweeps or multi-file refactoring, direct the primary agent to spawn concurrent background subagents handled by the agent manager. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/best-practices/

## Troubleshooting
<https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/>

- [parameter] **`agy: command not found`**: Potential Cause=Binary directory missing from shell environments.; Target Resolution=[Configure your shell PATH](https://antigravity.google#configure-your-shell-path) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#quick-reference
- [parameter] **`keyring: secure lock out`**: Potential Cause=Missing system service permissions or active lockouts.; Target Resolution=[Authorize keyring permissions](https://antigravity.google#authorize-keyring-permissions) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#quick-reference
- [parameter] **`SSH Clipboard paste failures`**: Potential Cause=Protocol streams blocked or missing forward configurations.; Target Resolution=[Enable emulator clipboard forwarding](https://antigravity.google#enable-emulator-clipboard-forwarding) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#quick-reference
- [parameter] **`Advisory lock / update failures`**: Potential Cause=Locked self-updater thread or read-only directory paths.; Target Resolution=[Resolve self-updater locks and failures](https://antigravity.google#resolve-self-updater-locks-and-failures) — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#quick-reference
- [definition] Troubleshooting — Diagnose and resolve common anomalies with installation PATHs, local self-updating locks, keyring access permissions, and SSH clipboard forwarding. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#troubleshooting
- [definition] Quick reference — Scan the lookup table below to identify symptoms and access immediate solutions: — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#quick-reference
- [definition] Symptom — Executing `agy` returns a shell terminal error: `bash: agy: command not found` The installation utility downloads the binary to `~/.local/bin` (or `C:\Users\<username>\AppData\Local\agy\bin`), but your shell’s active `$PATH` environment does not index this directory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#symptom
- [definition] Resolution — Ensure your terminal session loads the binary path. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#resolution
- [definition] Symptom — When launching, the CLI hangs, prints DBUS warnings, or throws keyring access exceptions: `Error: failed to retrieve token: secret keyring is locked` Antigravity CLI utilizes secure keychain libraries (Apple Keychain, Linux secret-service via dbus, or Windows Credential Manager) to encrypt your… — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#symptom
- [definition] Resolution — **macOS**: 1. Open **Keychain Access** app. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#resolution
- [definition] Symptom — Pasting screenshots or media files via `Ctrl+V` within an SSH terminal returns a failure notification: `Error: local pasteboard is empty or unreachable over SSH connection` Standard SSH streams do not forward graphical clipboards. Graphic uploads require specific terminal multiplexer protocols. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#symptom
- [definition] Resolution — Verify that you are utilizing supported terminal emulators and configurations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#resolution
- [definition] Symptom — Launching `agy` hangs, fails to apply upgrades, or returns an advisory lock warning: `Warning: another background updater process is already active (update.lock)` Antigravity CLI contains a native, statically linked self-updater that runs in the background. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#symptom
- [definition] Next steps — Access our quick reference sheets or configure advanced permissions: - **[CLI Reference](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference)** : Dense tables listing all slash commands and visual settings keys. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/troubleshooting/#next-steps

- [parameter] **`altScreenMode`**: Value Type=string; System Default=`"default"`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Screen buffer usage: `"default"` (adaptive inline/altscreen),`"always"` (force alternate screen buffer), or`"never"` (force inline sequential output). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`toolPermission`**: Value Type=string; System Default=`"request-review"`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Global safety presets: `"request-review"` (prompts for write/bash/web tools),`"proceed-in-sandbox"` (auto-proceed inside sandbox),`"always-proceed"` (never prompts), or`"strict"` (prompts for all non-read tools). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`artifactReviewPolicy`**: Value Type=string; System Default=`"asks-for-review"`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Code review policy: `"asks-for-review"` (always prompts before writing code),`"agent-decides"` (prompts dynamically), or`"always-proceed"` (never prompts). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson

- [parameter] **`showTips`**: Value Type=boolean; System Default=`true`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Displays helpful agentic tips above the prompt panel during generation turns. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`showFeedbackSurvey`**: Value Type=boolean; System Default=`true`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Displays periodic quality feedback surveys upon active task completions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`editor`**: Value Type=string; System Default=`"auto"`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Target text editor utility: `"auto"` (consults system`$EDITOR` ),`"vim"` ,`"emacs"` , or custom text labels. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`editorMode`**: Value Type=string; System Default=`"default"`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Prompt editing model: `"default"` (flat text editing) or`"vim"` (modal editing). Distinct from`editor` , which selects an external program. See[Vim Editor Mode](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/vim-editor-mode) . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`vimInsertFirst`**: Value Type=boolean; System Default=`false`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Starts Vim editing in Insert mode and makes a bare `Enter` submit. Requires`editorMode` set to`"vim"` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`allowNonWorkspaceAccess`**: Value Type=boolean; System Default=`false`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Permits the agent’s file read and write tools to navigate outside recognized Git/workspace roots. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`enableTerminalSandbox`**: Value Type=boolean; System Default=`false`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Restricts all local execution commands launched by agents to OS containment rings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`useG1Credits`**: Value Type=boolean; System Default=`false`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=*External builds only.* Uses personal AI credits for model calls once plan quotas are exhausted. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`enableTelemetry`**: Value Type=boolean; System Default=`true`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Permits metric collection and crash log streaming to improve tool reliability. — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`verbosity`**: Value Type=string; System Default=`"high"`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Visual verbosity level: `"high"` (renders full thoughts and tool outputs) or`"low"` (displays only minimal visual progress indicators). — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson
- [parameter] **`runningLightSpeed`**: Value Type=string; System Default=`"medium"`; Parameter Characteristics & Options=Visual running light progress animation speed: `"fast"` ,`"medium"` ,`"slow"` , or`"off"` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/reference/#example-settingsjson

## Personas and system instructions
<https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/personas/>

- [snippet] Templated system instructions: from google.antigravity import Agent, LocalAgentConfig, types — `from google.antigravity import Agent, LocalAgentConfig, types` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/personas/#templated-system-instructions
- [snippet] Custom system instructions: from google.antigravity import Agent, LocalAgentConfig, types — `from google.antigravity import Agent, LocalAgentConfig, types` — https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/personas/#custom-system-instructions
- [definition] Personas and system instructions — The SDK allows you to customize your agent’s identity, behavioral constraints, and instructions using templated or custom system instructions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/personas/#personas-and-system-instructions
- [definition] Templated system instructions — Templated system instructions are recommended for most applications. This approach preserves default SDK scaffolding and environment context while overriding the persona identity and appending custom structured sections. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/personas/#templated-system-instructions
- [definition] Custom system instructions — For complete control, custom system instructions bypass all default SDK scaffolding and environment context, allowing you to pass raw system prompts directly. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/personas/#custom-system-instructions

- [actionable] Use `run_interactive_loop` combined with `policy.safe_defaults` to start a console session that prompts for user approval. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/policies/
- [fact] The interactive loop prompts for user approval whenever any write tool (file creation, editing, or shell execution) is requested. — https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/policies/

## overview
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/>

- [concept] Antigravity IDE is an agentic development environment designed for the agent-first era that integrates agents directly into the workspace. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [concept] Agents in Antigravity operate autonomously across the editor, terminal, and browser with a focus on verification and high-level communication via tasks and artifacts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [fact] The IDE supports asynchronous, local agents that can work in parallel across all workspaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [concept] The Browser Agent is an agent capability designed to actuate the browser for tasks such as dashboard reads, SCM actions, and UI testing. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [fact] The Editor component is a fully-functional AI-powered IDE that maps to a single workspace. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [concept] Editor Tab Autocomplete is the real-time editing AI modality within the text surface, providing high-speed intelligent code completion and helper suggestions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [fact] Artifacts are structured deliverables created by agents to communicate accomplishments, including rich markdown plans, code diff views, architecture diagrams, images, and browser recordings. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [concept] The Agent & Parallel Subagents modality allows developers to spin up multiple parallel agents across multiple codebases while working with an agent in the Editor. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/
- [idea] Developers should leverage the ability for agents to plan and execute complex, end-to-end software tasks ranging from feature building to bug fixing and report generation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/overview/

## Getting Started
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/>

- [definition] Download — Please visit [antigravity.google/download](https://antigravity.google/download) to download Antigravity IDE. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/#download
- [fact] Antigravity IDE requires macOS versions with Apple security update support, typically the current and two previous versions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/
- [fact] The minimum supported version for macOS is 12 (Monterey). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/
- [problem] X86 architecture is not supported on macOS. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/
- [fact] Windows support requires Windows 10 (64 bit). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/
- [fact] Linux support requires glibc >= 2.28 and glibcxx >= 3.4.25. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/
- [fact] Supported Linux distributions include Ubuntu 20, Debian 10, Fedora 36, and RHEL 8. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/
- [actionable] Visit https://antigravity.google/download to download the Antigravity IDE. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/getting-started/

## Antigravity IDE: Tab & Navigation
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/tab/>

- [definition] Antigravity IDE: Tab & Navigation — This guide covers the core navigation and completion tools: **Supercomplete**, **Tab-to-Jump**, and **Tab-to-Import**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/tab/#antigravity-ide-tab-navigation
- [definition] Supercomplete — Supercomplete provides code suggestions in a region near your current cursor position. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/tab/#supercomplete
- [definition] Tab-to-Jump — Tab-to-Jump is a fluid navigation tool that suggests the next logical place in your document to move your cursor to. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/tab/#tab-to-jump
- [definition] Tab-to-Import — Tab-to-Import handles missing dependencies without breaking your flow. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/tab/#tab-to-import
- [definition] Settings — In your settings, you can customize the behavior of these features: - **Enable/Disable Features** : You can individually turn off Autocomplete, Tab-to-Jump, Supercomplete, or Tab-to-Import. - **Tab Speed** : Controls the responsiveness of suggestions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/tab/#settings

## Implementation Plan
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/>

- [definition] Implementation Plan — Agent utilizes the implementation plan artifact to architect changes within your codebase to accomplish a task. These plans contain technical details on what revisions are necessary and are meant to be reviewed by the user. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/#implementation-plan
- [concept] The implementation plan artifact is used by the Agent to architect codebase changes and contains technical details for user review. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/
- [fact] Unless the artifact review policy is set to 'Always Proceed', the Agent requests user review before making changes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/
- [actionable] Click the in-conversation or artifact header 'Proceed' button to instantly continue with the Agent's plan. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/
- [concept] Antigravity supports commenting on implementation plan artifacts to provide feedback on scope, tech stack, or discrepancies. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/
- [actionable] Toggle the 'Review' button in the artifact header to examine comments and leave a message instead of proceeding directly. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/
- [fact] After leaving comments or proceeding, the Agent either iterates on the plan re-requesting review or begins its work. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/implementation-plan/

## Walkthrough
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/walkthrough/>

- [definition] Walkthrough — Agent creates walkthrough artifacts when it has completed task implementation; this type of artifact includes a concise summary of the changes that have been made to remind the user of what has happened in the active conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/walkthrough/#walkthrough
- [concept] Agent creates walkthrough artifacts upon completing task implementation to summarize changes made during the active conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/walkthrough/
- [fact] For browser tasks, walkthroughs often contain screenshots and screen recordings of what Agent has built or created. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/walkthrough/

## Allowlist / Denylist
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/>

- [definition] Allowlist / Denylist — The browser uses a two-layer security system to control which URLs can be accessed: - **Denylist** - Deny dangerous/malicious URLs - **Allowlist** - Explicitly allow trusted URLs — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/#allowlist-denylist
- [definition] Denylist — The denylist is maintained and enforced using the Google Superroots’s BadUrlsChecker service (See documentation). When the browser attempts to navigate to a URL, the hostname is checked against the server-side denylist via RPC. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/#denylist
- [definition] Allowlist — The allowlist is a local text file that you can edit to explicitly trust specific URLs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/#allowlist
- [concept] The browser uses a two-layer security system consisting of a Denylist and an Allowlist to control URL access. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/
- [concept] The Denylist is maintained and enforced using the Google Superroots's BadUrlsChecker service. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/
- [fact] When a browser attempts to navigate, the hostname is checked against the server-side denylist via RPC. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/
- [problem] If the server is unavailable, access is denied by default. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/
- [concept] The Allowlist is a local text file that can be edited to explicitly trust specific URLs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/
- [fact] The allowlist is initialized with just localhost and can be edited at any time. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/
- [actionable] Click the 'always allow' button in the prompt to add a non-allowlisted URL to the allowlist. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/
- [problem] You cannot allowlist a URL that appears on the denylist because the denylist always takes precedence. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/allowlist-denylist/

## Model Context Protocol (MCP)
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/>

- [snippet] Antigravity SDK: import asyncio — `import asyncio` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#antigravity-sdk
- [snippet] MCP Configuration Structure: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#mcp-configuration-structure
- [snippet] Google Credentials: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#google-credentials
- [snippet] Google Credentials: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#google-credentials
- [snippet] Google Credentials: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#google-credentials
- [snippet] Custom Headers: { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#custom-headers
- [definition] Model Context Protocol (MCP) — Antigravity supports the [Model Context Protocol (MCP)](https://modelcontextprotocol.io), an open standard that lets AI agents and editors securely connect to local developer tools, databases, file parsers, and external remote APIs. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#model-context-protocol-mcp
- [definition] What is MCP? — MCP acts as a universal bridge between Antigravity and your broader development environment. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#what-is-mcp
- [definition] Add Context — With MCP, Antigravity can use live data from connected MCP servers to inform its reasoning and suggestions: - When writing a SQL query, Antigravity can inspect your live Neon, Supabase, or AlloyDB schema to suggest correct table and column names. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#add-context
- [definition] Add Custom Tools — With MCP, Antigravity can execute specific, safe actions defined by your connected servers: - Create a Linear issue for this TODO. - Search Notion or GitHub for authentication patterns. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#add-custom-tools
- [definition] Antigravity 2.0 — In Antigravity 2.0, you can manage your MCP servers through the **Installed MCP Servers** section of your **Settings**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#antigravity-20
- [definition] Antigravity IDE — In Antigravity IDE, the easiest way to manage MCP servers is through the built-in MCP Store. In the MCP Store, you can browse, discover, and install supported MCP servers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#antigravity-ide
- [definition] Antigravity CLI — Antigravity CLI supports both local `stdio` processes and remote host MCP server configurations. The simplest path to installing an MCP server on Antigravity CLI is by using the **Interactive MCP Manager**. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#antigravity-cli
- [definition] Interactive MCP Manager — Type `/mcp` inside the prompt panel and press `Enter` to open the interactive **MCP Manager Overlay**. This panel lets you: - View live status rings for active, disconnected, or loading servers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#interactive-mcp-manager
- [definition] Global and Workspace Server Configs — Unlike legacy setups, Antigravity CLI separates MCP definitions into dedicated, sparse configurations: - **Global server setups:** Configured in`~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` . - **Workspace local setups:** Configured in your active project under`.agents/mcp_config.json` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#global-and-workspace-server-configs
- [definition] Antigravity SDK — In Python applications built using the [Antigravity SDK](https://antigravity.google/docs/sdk/overview), MCP servers (`stdio`, `SSE`, or `HTTP`) can be connected programmatically under a unified execution pipeline alongside built-in tools and custom Python functions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#antigravity-sdk
- [definition] MCP Configuration Structure — Whether configuring custom servers for Antigravity 2.0, Antigravity IDE, or Antigravity CLI, the configuration file follows a standardized format. The file contains a single `mcpServers` object where you define each server you want to connect to: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#mcp-configuration-structure
- [definition] MCP Configuration Properties — Each server entry under `mcpServers` supports the following properties: **Transport (one required):** — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#mcp-configuration-properties
- [definition] MCP Authentication — Connected MCP servers can securely authenticate against external services using built-in Google credentials, automatic OAuth flows, or custom HTTP headers. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#mcp-authentication
- [definition] Google Credentials — Set `authProviderType` to `"google_credentials"` to use Google Application Default Credentials (ADC). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#google-credentials
- [definition] Custom Headers — For remote servers that require custom HTTP headers (e.g. API keys or bearer tokens), add them to the `headers` object. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#custom-headers
- [definition] MCP Permissions and Access Control — Access to Model Context Protocol tools and resources is governed by Antigravity’s [permissions system](https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions). By default, unconfigured MCP tools run in **Ask** mode, requiring your approval before execution. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#mcp-permissions-and-access-control
- [definition] Supported MCP Servers — The MCP Store features direct integrations for a wide variety of developer platforms, databases, and productivity services: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/mcp/#supported-mcp-servers
