- [parameter] `<workspace-root>/.agents/skills/<skill-folder>/`: Workspace-specific — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#where-skills-live
- [parameter] `~/.gemini/antigravity/skills/<skill-folder>/`: Global (all workspaces) — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#where-skills-live
- [parameter] `name`: Required=No; Description=A unique identifier for the skill (lowercase, hyphens for spaces). Defaults to the folder name if not provided. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#frontmatter-fields
- [parameter] `description`: Required=Yes; Description=A clear description of what the skill does and when to use it. This is what the agent sees when deciding whether to apply the skill. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#frontmatter-fields
- [definition] Agent Skills — Skills are an [open standard](https://agentskills.io/home) for extending agent capabilities. A skill is a folder containing a `SKILL.md` file with instructions that the agent can follow when working on specific tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#agent-skills
- [definition] What are skills? — Skills are reusable packages of knowledge that extend what the agent can do. Each skill contains: - **Instructions** for how to approach a specific type of task - **Best practices** and conventions to follow - **Optional scripts and resources** the agent can use — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#what-are-skills
- [definition] Where skills live — Antigravity supports two types of skills: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#where-skills-live
- [definition] Creating a skill — To create a skill: 1. Create a folder for your skill in one of the skill directories 2. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#creating-a-skill
- [definition] Skill folder structure — While `SKILL.md` is the only required file, you can include additional resources: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#skill-folder-structure
- [definition] How the agent uses skills — Skills follow a **progressive disclosure** pattern: 1. **Discovery** : When a conversation starts, the agent sees a list of available skills with their names and descriptions 2. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#how-the-agent-uses-skills
- [definition] Keep skills focused — Each skill should do one thing well. Instead of a “do everything” skill, create separate skills for distinct tasks. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#keep-skills-focused
- [definition] Write clear descriptions — The description is how the agent decides whether to use your skill. Make it specific about what the skill does and when it’s useful. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#write-clear-descriptions
- [definition] Use scripts as black boxes — If your skill includes scripts, encourage the agent to run them with `--help` first rather than reading the entire source code. This keeps the agent’s context focused on the task. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#use-scripts-as-black-boxes
- [definition] Include decision trees — For complex skills, add a section that helps the agent choose the right approach based on the situation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#include-decision-trees
- [definition] Example: A code review skill — Here’s a simple skill that helps the agent review code: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/#example-a-code-review-skill
- [concept] Skills are an open standard for extending agent capabilities, defined as folders containing a SKILL.md file with instructions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [fact] Antigravity defaults to the .agents/skills directory but maintains backward support for .agent/skills. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [concept] Workspace skills are designed for project-specific workflows like team deployment processes or testing conventions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [concept] Global skills work across all projects and are suitable for personal utilities or general-purpose tools. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [actionable] To create a skill, create a folder in a skill directory and add a SKILL.md file inside it. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [fact] Every skill requires a SKILL.md file containing YAML frontmatter at the top. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [idea] Write the skill description in the third person and include keywords to help the agent recognize relevance. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [concept] Skills follow a progressive disclosure pattern involving discovery, activation, and execution phases. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [fact] The agent decides whether to use a skill based on context without explicit instruction, though users can mention a skill by name to ensure usage. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [idea] Keep skills focused by ensuring each performs one specific task rather than attempting to do everything. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [idea] Write clear descriptions that are specific about what the skill does and when it is useful, as this drives agent decision-making. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [idea] Encourage agents to run scripts with --help first rather than reading source code to keep context focused on the task. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/
- [idea] For complex skills, include a decision tree section to help the agent choose the right approach based on the situation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/skills/

## Global Rules
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/>

- [definition] Global Rules — Global rules live in ~/.gemini/GEMINI.md and are applied across all workspaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/#global-rules
- [definition] Workspace Rules — Workspace rules live in the .agents/rules folder of your workspace or git root. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/#workspace-rules
- [definition] @ Mentions — You can reference other files using @filename in a Rules file. If filename is a relative path, it will be interpreted relative to the location of the Rules file. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/#mentions
- [concept] Rules are manually defined constraints for the Agent to follow at local and global levels. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] Rules files are limited to 12,000 characters each. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [actionable] Open the Customizations panel via the '…' dropdown at the top of the editor's agent panel to get started with Rules. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [actionable] Click + Global to create new Global Rules or + Workspace to create new Workspace-specific rules. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] Global rules live in ~/.gemini/GEMINI.md and are applied across all workspaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] Workspace rules live in the .agents/rules folder of your workspace or git root. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [concept] Rules can be defined to activate manually via at mention, always on, based on model decision, or via glob patterns. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] The Manual activation mode requires the rule to be manually activated via at mention in the Agent's input box. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] The Always On activation mode ensures the rule is always applied. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [concept] Model Decision activation allows the model to decide whether to apply a rule based on a natural language description. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] Glob activation applies rules to files matching a defined pattern, such as *.js or src/**/* .ts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] Antigravity now defaults to .agents/rules but maintains backward support for .agent/rules. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [concept] You can reference other files using @filename in a Rules file. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] If a filename is a relative path, it is interpreted relative to the location of the Rules file. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] If a filename is an absolute path, it is resolved as a true absolute path. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [fact] For non-absolute paths that are not relative to the Rules file, resolution defaults to being relative to the repository. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [problem] When using @/path/to/file.md, if the true absolute path does not exist, it resolves to workspace/path/to/file.md instead. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/
- [idea] Use Markdown files to input constraints that guide the Agent to your specific tasks, stack, and style. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/rules/

## Workflows
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/workflows/>

- [definition] Workflows — Workflows enable you to define a series of steps to guide the Agent through a repetitive set of tasks, such as deploying a service or responding to PR comments. These Workflows are saved as markdown files, allowing you to have an easy repeatable way to run key processes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/workflows/#workflows
- [definition] Agent-Generated Workflows — You can also ask Agent to generate Workflows for you! This works particularly well after manually working with Agent through a series of steps since it can use the conversation history to create the Workflow. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/workflows/#agent-generated-workflows

## Plugins
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/>

- [snippet] Directory Structure: plugins/<plugin-name>/ — `plugins/<plugin-name>/` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#directory-structure
- [snippet] Manifest File (`plugin.json`): { — `{` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#manifest-file-pluginjson
- [definition] Plugins — Plugins are namespaced bundles that allow you to extend Antigravity’s capabilities by grouping skills, rules, MCP servers, and hooks into a single package. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#plugins
- [definition] Directory Structure — If you want to create your own plugins or inspect existing ones, they follow a specific directory structure. A plugin is a directory containing a `plugin.json` file and optional subdirectories for different customization types: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#directory-structure
- [definition] Manifest File (`plugin.json`) — Every plugin must have a `plugin.json` file at its root. This file identifies the directory as a plugin. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#manifest-file-pluginjson
- [definition] Supported Components — A plugin can contain the following components: 1. **Skills** : Located in the`skills/` subdirectory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#supported-components
- [definition] How to Add Plugins — There are two ways to add plugins to Antigravity: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#how-to-add-plugins
- [definition] 1. Using Bundled Plugins (Build with Google) — Antigravity comes with a variety of bundled plugins created by Google. You can browse and add these plugins directly from the user interface: - Navigate to the **Customizations** page. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#1-using-bundled-plugins-build-with-google
- [definition] 2. Manually Adding Plugins — You can also add custom plugins by placing your plugin folders in one of the designated plugin locations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/#2-manually-adding-plugins
- [concept] Plugins are namespaced bundles that group skills, rules, MCP servers, and hooks into a single package to extend Antigravity's capabilities. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] A plugin is a directory containing a `plugin.json` file at its root and optional subdirectories for customization types. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [concept] The `name` field in `plugin.json` is optional; if omitted, it defaults to the directory name. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] Skills are located in the `skills/` subdirectory and each must contain a `SKILL.md` file with instructions for the agent. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] Rules are markdown files located in the `rules/` subdirectory that define constraints or guidelines for the agent's behavior. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] MCP Servers are configured via a `mcp_config.json` file at the plugin root to connect Antigravity to external tools and services. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] Hooks are configured via a `hooks.json` file at the plugin root to run scripts or commands when specific events occur. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [actionable] Navigate to the **Customizations** page in the user interface to browse and add bundled plugins created by Google. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] Workspace-level plugins are added by placing a plugin folder inside `.agents/plugins/` or `_agents/plugins/` at the root of the opened workspace. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] Global-level plugins are added by placing a plugin folder inside `~/.gemini/config/plugins/` in the user home directory to make them active across all workspaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [idea] Use the **Build with Google Page** documentation for more details about available Google-built plugins. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/
- [fact] Antigravity automatically scans designated directories to discover and load custom plugin folders. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/plugins/

- [parameter] `enabled`: Type=boolean; Description=Optional. Set to `false` to disable the hook without removing it. Defaults to`true` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PreToolUse`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run before a tool is executed. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PostToolUse`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run after a tool completes. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PreInvocation`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run before Antigravity calls the model. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PostInvocation`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run after tool calls finish. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `Stop`: Type=array; Description=Handlers that run when the execution loop terminates. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-definition-fields
- [parameter] `PreToolUse`: Description=Fires before a tool is executed.; Matcher Target=Tool name (e.g., `run_command` ) — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `PostToolUse`: Description=Fires after a tool completes.; Matcher Target=Tool name — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `PreInvocation`: Description=Fires before the model is called.; Matcher Target=N/A (matcher ignored) — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `PostInvocation`: Description=Fires after tool calls finish.; Matcher Target=N/A (matcher ignored) — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `Stop`: Description=Fires when execution terminates.; Matcher Target=N/A (matcher ignored) — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#supported-events
- [parameter] `type`: Type=string; Description=Optional. Currently only `"command"` is supported. Defaults to`"command"` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [parameter] `command`: Type=string; Description=Required. The shell command to execute. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [parameter] `timeout`: Type=integer; Description=Optional. Timeout in seconds. Defaults to `30` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [parameter] `conversationId`: Type=string; Description=The unique UUID of the active agent conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `workspacePaths`: Type=array of strings; Description=Absolute directory paths representing the user’s mounted workspaces. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `transcriptPath`: Type=string; Description=The absolute path to the persistent `transcript.jsonl` conversation logs.**Note** : This file lives in:`<app_data_dir>/brain/<conversationId>/.system_generated/logs/transcript.jsonl` where`<app_data_dir>` is`~/.gemini/antigravity-ide` — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `artifactDirectoryPath`: Type=string; Description=The absolute path to the directory containing all conversation artifacts and screenshots. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [parameter] `toolCall`: Type=object; Description=Details of the proposed tool call. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `toolCall.name`: Type=string; Description=The name of the tool being executed (e.g., `run_command` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `toolCall.args`: Type=object; Description=The arguments passed to the tool. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `stepIdx`: Type=integer; Description=The 0-based index of the current step in the trajectory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `decision`: Type=string; Description=**Required.** Controls how the tool call is gated:- `"allow"` : Automatically allows the tool execution.- `"deny"` : Hard blocks execution immediately.- `"ask"` : Prompts the user, but respects “Always Allow” settings.- `"force_ask"` : Always prompts the user, ignoring cached permissions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `reason`: Type=string; Description=**Optional.** The explanation shown to the agent or user for the decision. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `permissionOverrides`: Type=array of strings; Description=**Optional.** A list of resource strings (e.g.`["read_file(/path)", "command(args)"]` ) to override default tool permissions. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#pretooluse
- [parameter] `stepIdx`: Type=integer; Description=The 0-based index of the completed step. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#posttooluse
- [parameter] `error`: Type=string; Description=Optional. The detailed runtime error message if the tool call failed. Empty if successful. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#posttooluse
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#posttooluse
- [parameter] `invocationNum`: Type=integer; Description=The 0-indexed sequence number of the current model invocation (the first invocation is 0). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] `initialNumSteps`: Type=integer; Description=The number of steps currently in the trajectory. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] `injectSteps`: Type=array of objects; Description=**Optional.** List of steps to inject into the conversation trajectory before the model is called. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#preinvocation
- [parameter] `injectSteps`: Type=array of objects; Description=**Optional.** List of steps to inject after the invocation completes (same schema as`PreInvocation` inject steps). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `terminationBehavior`: Type=string; Description=**Optional.** Controls the execution flow after injection:- `"force_continue"` : Forces the loop to continue.- `"terminate"` : Forces the loop to terminate.- `""` (or omitted): Default behavior. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `executionNum`: Type=integer; Description=The sequence number of the execution attempt. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `terminationReason`: Type=string; Description=The reason why the execution is stopping (e.g., `"model_stop"` ,`"max_steps_exceeded"` ,`"error"` ). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `error`: Type=string; Description=Optional. The error message if termination was caused by a system error. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `fullyIdle`: Type=boolean; Description=**Required.**`true` if the agent is completely finished and all background commands or asynchronous tasks have completed.`false` if active background tasks are still running. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] *(Common Fields)*: Description=Includes `conversationId` ,`workspacePaths` ,`transcriptPath` ,`artifactDirectoryPath` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `decision`: Type=string; Description=**Required.** Set to`"continue"` to prevent the agent from stopping and re-enter the execution loop. Any other value allows the stop. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [parameter] `reason`: Type=string; Description=**Optional.** If`decision` is`"continue"` , this message is injected as a system message into the conversation. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#postinvocation
- [definition] Configuration — Hooks are configured in a `hooks.json` file located in your customization directory (e.g., `.agents/` in your workspace or `~/.gemini/config/`). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#configuration
- [definition] Schema and File Format — The `hooks.json` file maps hook names to their event configurations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#schema-and-file-format
- [definition] Matcher — For `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse`, you can use a regular expression in the `matcher` field to specify which tools trigger the hook: - `""` or`"*"` : Match all tools. - `"run_command"` : Match exactly`run_command` . — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#matcher
- [definition] Supported Tools — For `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse` matchers, you can match against the following tool names, grouped by category: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#supported-tools
- [definition] Hook Handler Configuration — Each item in the `hooks` array supports: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#hook-handler-configuration
- [definition] Input/Output Contract — Hooks receive input via **stdin** as JSON and should return output via **stdout** as JSON. Field names use camelCase. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#inputoutput-contract
- [definition] Common Input Fields — All hooks receive the following system metadata fields in their input payload on `stdin`: — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#common-input-fields
- [definition] PreInvocation — Fires before the model is called (starts at 0). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/hooks/#preinvocation

## Settings
<https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/>

- [definition] Settings — Configure how the Antigravity Agent interacts with your environment, executes commands, and secures your workspace. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#settings
- [definition] Terminal Command Auto Execution — Controls how the agent executes generated shell commands: - **Request Review** : The agent will always prompt for confirmation before executing any terminal command (except those explicitly added to your configurable Allow list). — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#terminal-command-auto-execution
- [definition] Agent Non-Workspace File Access — Allows the agent to view and edit files outside of the active project folders. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#agent-non-workspace-file-access
- [definition] Strict Mode — Strict mode provides enhanced security controls for the Agent, allowing you to restrict its access to external resources and sensitive operations. When strict mode is enabled, several security measures are enforced to protect your environment. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#strict-mode
- [definition] Browser URL Allowlist/Denylist — In strict mode, the Agent’s ability to interact with external websites is governed by the browser’s Allowlist and Denylist. This applies to: - **External Markdown Images** : The Agent will only render images from URLs that are allowed. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#browser-url-allowlistdenylist
- [definition] Terminal, Browser, and Artifact Review Policies — Strict mode enforces the following behavior for terminal, browser, and artifact interactions: - **Terminal Auto Execution** : Set to “Request Review”. The Agent will always prompt for permission before executing any terminal command. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#terminal-browser-and-artifact-review-policies
- [definition] File System Access — Strict mode restricts the Agent’s access to the file system to ensure it only interacts with authorized files: - **Respect .gitignore** : The Agent will respect`.gitignore` rules, preventing it from accessing ignored files. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#file-system-access
- [definition] Terminal Sandboxing — Sandboxing provides kernel-level isolation for terminal commands executed by the Agent. When enabled, commands run in a restricted environment with limited file system and network access, protecting your system from unintended modifications. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#terminal-sandboxing
- [definition] Enabling Sandboxing — You can enable or disable sandboxing in Antigravity User Settings. Toggle “Enable Terminal Sandboxing” to turn sandboxing on or off. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#enabling-sandboxing
- [definition] Restrictions — When sandboxing is enabled, the Agent’s terminal commands are subject to the following restrictions: - **File System** : Commands can only write to your designated workspace directory and essential system locations. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#restrictions
- [definition] Interaction with Strict Mode — When strict mode is enabled, sandboxing is automatically activated with network access denied. This ensures maximum protection when operating in a strict environment. — https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/settings/#interaction-with-strict-mode

## firebase-studio-migration
<https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/>

- [concept] Antigravity is Google's next-generation, agent-first platform designed as a local IDE that brings AI-powered autonomous development workflows to your machine. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [concept] The platform provides true agentic development by autonomously formatting, testing, and implementing tasks across the codebase rather than just offering code completion. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [fact] Antigravity requires Node.js version 20 or higher and Firebase CLI version 15.10.0 or higher to be installed locally. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [fact] The recommended model for optimizing workflow and conserving tokens during high-volume transformation tasks is Gemini Flash. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [actionable] To initiate automated migration in Firebase Studio, click the Move now button at the top of your workspace. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [actionable] If the Zip and Download button is not visible, open the command palette using Cmd + Shift + P on Mac or Ctrl + Shift + P on ChromeOS, Windows, or Linux, then run the Firebase Studio: Zip & Download command. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [actionable] To start the migration agent, enter the prompt @fbs-to-agy-export in the Agent pane within Antigravity after opening your extracted project folder. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [actionable] For manual export, run npx firebase-tools@latest studio:export <path> in your terminal, replacing <path> with the file path to your extracted project folder or original .zip file. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [actionable] To view your application locally, navigate to the Run and Debug menu in the left sidebar of Antigravity and click the play button. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [actionable] To publish your app, enter the prompt Publish my app in the chat panel and choose Yes when prompted to run firebase deploy. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [fact] When publishing for the first time, the agent will walk you through the process; for subsequent updates, it publishes to your existing URL automatically. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [fact] You can execute workflows in Antigravity by typing @workflows <workflow_name> into the agentic chat panel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [problem] If you experience deployment issues, you may need to re-authenticate with the Firebase CLI or verify your project secrets. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [idea] Antigravity supports seamless Firebase integration by allowing you to deploy projects, communicate via the Firebase CLI, and test functions locally as in Firebase Studio. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/
- [idea] For faster offline testing, run the Firebase Local Emulator Suite directly on your machine instead of using browser-based emulators. — https://antigravity.google/docs/firebase-studio-migration/

## Baseline Quota
<https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/>

- [definition] Baseline Quota — All plans receive a baseline of: - Use of Gemini models including Gemini 3.1 Pro, Gemini 3.5 Flash, and other offered Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform models as the core agent model - Unlimited Tab completions - Access to all product features, such as the Scheduled Tasks and the CLI — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/#baseline-quota
- [definition] Overages — Users on Google AI Pro or Ultra plans can utilize [purchased AI credits](http://one.google.com/ai/credits) (or any one-time promotional credits) for additional overage usage above the baseline provided quota. AI credits are consumed at standard Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform consumption pricing. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/#overages
- [concept] Google Antigravity is available to individual accounts under Google's terms of service and to teams via the Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform under Google Cloud terms. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [fact] Rate limits and model availability differ based on usage of Google AI plans. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [concept] All plans include a baseline quota featuring Gemini models (Gemini 3.1 Pro, Gemini 3.5 Flash) as the core agent model. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [fact] All plans provide unlimited Tab completions and access to all product features like Scheduled Tasks and the CLI. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [concept] Google AI Ultra users receive the highest quota, refreshed every five hours, along with the highest weekly rate limits and access to third-party models. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [concept] Google AI Pro users receive a high quota refreshed every five hours until the weekly limit is reached, with higher weekly rate limits. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [fact] Users not on AI Pro or Ultra plans receive a meaningful quota refreshed weekly with a standard weekly rate limit. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [concept] Baseline rate limits are correlated with the amount of work done by the agent, meaning straightforward tasks may allow more prompts than complex ones. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [problem] Usage limits for this service are subject to modification to manage system capacity and maintain stability. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [actionable] Users on Google AI Pro or Ultra plans can purchase AI credits via the provided link for overage usage above the baseline quota. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [concept] AI credits are consumed at standard Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform consumption pricing when used for overages. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [actionable] Users can configure the 'AI Credit Overages' setting to control credit usage after baseline quota exhaustion. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [fact] Setting AI Credit Overages to 'Never' prevents automatic credit use, requiring users to wait for the baseline quota refresh. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [fact] Setting AI Credit Overages to 'Always' automatically uses credits when the baseline is exhausted, switching back upon refresh. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [actionable] Baseline quota usage across models can be viewed in the settings page. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [problem] There is currently no support for bring-your-own-key or bring-your-own-endpoint to increase rate limits. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/
- [problem] Organizational tiers via contract are not currently supported for Google Antigravity. — https://antigravity.google/docs/plans/

## Why can I not authenticate into Google Antigravity?
<https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/>

- [definition] Why can I not authenticate into Google Antigravity? — Google Antigravity is currently available for personal Google accounts in approved geographies. Please try using an @gmail.com email address if having challenges with Workspace Google accounts. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#why-can-i-not-authenticate-into-google-antigravity
- [definition] Why is my age unverified? — At the moment, Antigravity is unavailable to under-18 users. If you do meet the minimum age requirement, you may [verify your age](https://myaccount.google.com/age-verification) to continue using Antigravity. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#why-is-my-age-unverified
- [definition] What is Google Antigravity’s geographical availability? — Google Antigravity is available in the following countries and territories. If you’re not in one of these countries or territories, you will be unable to use Google Antigravity at this time: — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#what-is-google-antigravitys-geographical-availability
- [definition] Why am I ineligible for a Google One AI plan? — The following regions do not currently have access to Google One AI plans: - Democratic Republic of the Congo - Eswatini - Falkland Islands (Islas Malvinas) - Faroe Islands - Greenland - Guernsey - Heard Island and McDonald Islands - Saint Helena, Ascension and Tristan da Cunha - Saint Kitts and… — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#why-am-i-ineligible-for-a-google-one-ai-plan
- [definition] What is Google Antigravity’s stance on data collection? — Please refer to the [Terms of Service](https://antigravity.google/terms). You may opt out of data collection at any point from the Settings panel. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#what-is-google-antigravitys-stance-on-data-collection
- [definition] How do I sign in with a GCP project? — Follow the steps in the [Enterprise Page](https://antigravity.google/docs/enterprise) to learn how to sign in with GCP. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#how-do-i-sign-in-with-a-gcp-project
- [definition] How do I get support? — Check out the communities on our [Support page](https://antigravity.google/support). — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#how-do-i-get-support
- [definition] What are the model rate limits? — Please see more details in the docs on [Plans](https://antigravity.google/docs/plans). — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#what-are-the-model-rate-limits
- [definition] Why can’t I use third party software (e.g. Claude Code, OpenClaw, OpenCode) with my Antigravity login? — Using third party software, tools, or services to access Antigravity is a violation of our [Terms of Service](https://antigravity.google/terms), and severely degrades the experience for legitimate product users. Such actions may be grounds for suspension or termination of your account. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#why-cant-i-use-third-party-software-eg-claude-code-openclaw-opencode-with-my-antigravity-login
- [definition] Does Google Antigravity currently support worktrees? — Yes, you can use worktrees in Antigravity 2.0. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#does-google-antigravity-currently-support-worktrees
- [definition] What happens when my computer goes to sleep? — If an agent is running, Antigravity will prevent your computer from sleeping. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/#what-happens-when-my-computer-goes-to-sleep
- [fact] Google Antigravity is currently available for personal Google accounts in approved geographies. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/
- [problem] Users with Workspace Google accounts may face authentication challenges and should try using an @gmail.com email address instead. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/
- [fact] Antigravity is unavailable to users under the age of 18. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/
- [actionable] Users meeting the minimum age requirement may verify their age at https://myaccount.google.com/age-verification to continue using Antigravity. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/
- [fact] Google Antigravity is available in specific countries and territories across the Americas, Europe, Africa, Asia, Oceania, and Antarctica. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/
- [problem] Users located outside of approved countries or territories will be unable to use Google Antigravity at this time. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/
- [fact] The following regions do not currently have access to Google One AI plans: Democratic Republic of the Congo, Eswatini, Falkland Islands (Islas Malvinas), Faroe Islands, Greenland, Guernsey, Heard Island and McDonald Islands, Saint Helena, Ascension and Tristan da Cunha, Saint Kitts and Nevis, Saint Vincent and the Grenadines, São Tomé and Príncipe, South Georgia and the South Sandwich Islands, United States Minor Outlying Islands, U.S. Virgin Islands, Wallis and Futuna. — https://antigravity.google/docs/faq/

## Antigravity SDK
<https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk>

- [definition] Antigravity SDK — Build AI agents that autonomously read files, run commands, edit code, and more. The Agent SDK gives you the same tools, agent loop, and context management that power Google Antigravity, programmable in Python. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk#antigravity-sdk
- [definition] Important — The Google Antigravity SDK relies on a compiled runtime binary that is included in the platform-specific wheels published to [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/google-antigravity/). Cloning this repository alone is not sufficient to run the SDK. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk#important
- [definition] Unified Tool Experience — Layer custom Python callables, Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers, and reusable agent skills over our built-in filesystem and terminal tools under a single pipeline. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk#unified-tool-experience
- [definition] Focus on Customizing — The SDK abstracts away the complex machinery of running an AI agent—including state management, tool execution, and backend communication—allowing developers to focus on the agent's behavior rather than infrastructure. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk#focus-on-customizing
- [definition] Multimodal Ingestion — Pass rich multimedia file attachments (images, videos, audio, and documents) to the agent alongside textual instruction prompt lists. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk#multimodal-ingestion
- [concept] The Antigravity SDK is a Python library that provides the tools, agent loop, and context management used by Google Antigravity for building autonomous AI agents. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk
- [fact] The SDK relies on a compiled runtime binary included in platform-specific wheels published to PyPI. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk
- [problem] Cloning the repository alone is not sufficient to run the SDK because it requires the binary from the installation wheel. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk
- [actionable] Install the SDK using the command pip install google-antigravity to obtain the necessary binary. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk
- [concept] The Unified Tool Experience allows layering custom Python callables, MCP servers, and reusable skills over built-in filesystem and terminal tools. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk
- [concept] The SDK abstracts state management, tool execution, and backend communication to let developers focus on agent behavior. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk
- [fact] Multimodal ingestion supports passing images, videos, audio, and documents alongside textual instruction prompt lists. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk
- [actionable] Attach assets directly using content classes for in-memory bytes or from a filesystem path to automatically resolve types and guess MIME formats. — https://antigravity.google/product/antigravity-sdk

## Why Marketers choose Google Antigravity
<https://antigravity.google/use-cases/marketer>

- [definition] Why Marketers choose Google Antigravity — Google Antigravity removes the bottlenecks of daily work and accelerates execution. Build custom skills, run workflows in parallel, and steer agents with real-time, iterative feedback. — https://antigravity.google/use-cases/marketer#why-marketers-choose-google-antigravity
